## [2026-07-31] build | the strike force: withheld from the engagement, or the dump never leaves the first fight
Artifacts: `src/rw_bot/policy/{rush,campaign}.py`, gate green
Notes: The income-aimed dump was wiped with its dip unmoved (2,250), and the autopsy named the third and deepest gap in the all-in chain: the march order is issued ONCE, but the engagement policy re-tasks every released unit onto visible targets every tick -- the moment a marcher met the army mid-map it stopped being a marcher. Aim was never the problem; ownership was. The raid verb already solves this for its three-unit party by withholding it from the waves, so the all-in now does the same at scale: once the controller is committed, every marched unit leaves the engagement pool -- `fighting` minus `rusher.ordered()` -- and its attack-move carries it through contact toward the pool it was dealt ([[policy-combat]]). The commitment tick itself may still engage (the flag is read before the command that sets it, and the march waypoint lands last and wins in the engine); every tick after, fresh hostiles are ignored, and that is the pin. Tonight's chain, each step measured and each fix pinned: released-but-never-marched, marched-but-fought-the-army, aimed-but-re-tasked -- and now: released on time, aimed at income, owned by nobody but the walk.

## [2026-07-31] measure | gap four is arithmetic, and the last untouched lever is the clock
Artifacts: `runs/allin600d.out`, `runs/lurk-fast.out` (in flight)
Notes: The complete strike force -- released on time, aimed at income posts, withheld from the engagement -- was wiped at 1,939, the earliest death of the family: 27 units walked away and home fell 700 samples sooner, while the strikers still never touched a pool (dip 2,550). The all-in chain closed all three mechanical gaps and exposed the arithmetic one beneath them: **by any trigger we can afford, our production covers home defence OR a strike, never both, and Impossible requires both.** Fifty-plus matches at this rung have now refuted every composition, posture, timing and targeting available at lockstep 75. What no arm has ever varied is the cadence itself: every micro-dependent mechanism died of once-per-75-frames reactions -- the lurker's flee, the retreat that never existed, the kiting nothing attempts. `PLAY_LOCKSTEP` is a parameter. The lurk arm is re-probing at lockstep 25 -- triple the decision rate, the flee checked three times as often -- as the first measurement anywhere off the 75-frame baseline.

## [2026-07-31] measure | triple cadence does not save the lurker, and the map dimension opens
Artifacts: `runs/lurk-fast.out`, `runs/bridge-{tech,cover}.out` (in flight)
Notes: The lockstep-25 lurk probe: defeated, dip unchanged, fourteen scout deaths against twenty-one at standard cadence -- the faster clock softened the treadmill without flipping it, because an interceptor is faster than a scout at ANY reaction rate. The cadence lever is measured and closed as a lurk-reviver (it may yet matter for army kiting, unprobed). What has never been varied in fifty-plus Impossible matches is the terrain: every match ran on open duel_lake, the map shape the community's turtle-and-counter meta is WEAKEST on. The shipped pool carries real chokes -- Fire Bridge funnels every ground army across a bridge -- and the champion plus its turtle variant are probing there now. The bot's geometry is map-agnostic by construction (anchor, pool centroid, mirror), so this is a parameter change, not a build.

## [2026-07-31] measure | the bridge probes: terrain helps the trade, and names the missing verb
Artifacts: `runs/bridge-{tech2,cover2}.out`, `.game/assets/maps/skirmish/[p2]{fire_bridge,dire_straight}.tmx` (space-free copies; spaces in a map name break the launcher's quoting and the agent never loads)
Notes: First matches of the campaign off duel_lake. Fire Bridge at Impossible: the champion defeated at 3,065 (dip 4,350), the turtle variant at 2,801 with **dip 7,750 -- the second-deepest dent of the fifty-seven-match Impossible ledger**. The choke pays, and the autopsy says why it does not pay enough: **the wall was built at the base, not at the bridge.** Cover sites turrets on the anchor ring by construction, so the funnel the map offers stood unmanned while their army crossed it freely and fought at our door. The community meta this map rewards stands AT the choke -- turrets and repair bays on the bridge mouth, artillery behind it out-ranging everything that enters the funnel. The pieces exist separately tonight: the creep verb already walks turrets forward stepwise (refuted as an attack, unmeasured as a HOLD), repairbay is plan-expressible (fortress arm), and c_artillery out-ranges the funnel. The missing verb is the hold-point: creep to the midpoint and stop, fortify there, arty behind. That is the next build, and the first one aimed by terrain rather than against it.

## [2026-07-31] build | the hold-point wall, and the loop splits into its two honest halves
Artifacts: `src/rw_bot/policy/{creep,dispatching,campaign}.py`, `tests/test_architecture.py` (two-module loop), `doctrines/bastion.doctrine`, gate green
Notes: Two builds. **The creep verb holds now**: `creep` is a percent of the anchor-to-enemy line rather than a flag, the walk stops within half a step of its hold point, and every third structure it lays is a repair bay -- walked to one hundred it died at the enemy's door (the 164-turret refutation), held at a choke it is the community's whole answer, and the bridge autopsy showed our wall standing at the base while the funnel it should have manned went unmanned. **And the loop split into its two honest halves** on Austin's modularity directive: `campaign.py` (415 lines) perceives, arbitrates and orders the tick; `dispatching.py` (~240) is the loop's sending arm, every order leaving through it; the architecture guard now names exactly that pair -- the campaign may not send, a pure module may not either. The squeeze-comments-under-600 era is over. `bastion.doctrine` (creep 50 + repair cycle + aa_cover + mass 40 + tech on the champion economy) probes fire_bridge at Impossible now: the wall AT the bridge, the horde behind it.

## [2026-07-31] measure | the bastion pair: the wall starves the army or the army starves the wall
Artifacts: `runs/bastion{,2}.out`, `src/rw_bot/policy/creep.py` (saving wall, pinned)
Notes: Two probes, one clean trade-off. v1 (walk stops at first refusal): the 1,500-credit repair bay headed the sequential walk and was never affordable in a tick -- `creep:repairbay asked 2,378 got 0` -- so the wall stood at two turrets all match, the same head-of-queue starvation the siege arm died of, repeated in code written the same day it was first measured. v2 (a refused wall piece withholds its price, the tech unlock's gated pattern): **the wall built -- eight turrets, three repair bays, 8,500 credits of masonry at the bridge -- and the match ended earlier** (defeated 2,181 vs 2,947), because the wall's saving pauses bind the protected replacement claims behind them and the horde thinned exactly as the wall thickened. Dips 3,400 / 3,850; their army 122,600-177,650. The community meta, executed whole on its own terrain, states the same arithmetic every arm has: **at 3.7x, any static allocation between wall and army is beaten by an opponent who needs no such choice.** The machinery is right and pinned -- the hold-point walk, the healing cycle, the saving wall -- and what it measures is the rung, not a bug.

## [2026-07-31] research+build | the community sweep lands three findings, and the decoy verb ships
Artifacts: `wiki/sources/m17-community/` (Steam guides x2, Steam cheating-thread, NamuWiki unit meta, fandom pages -- captured locally and read mechanically), `src/rw_bot/policy/decoy.py`, `doctrines/{scatter,legion}.doctrine`, gate green
Notes: Austin's research directive, run under the tech-research capture discipline. Three findings with teeth. **(1) Every arm we have ever fielded had zero healing, and healing is the community meta's spine**: the winning guide spams fabricator-class support behind a 100-300 tank mass with a constant reinforcement stream; NamuWiki names the combat engineer -- armed, 1,000hp, heals units at twice the repair bay's rate -- the mid-game staple, "produces a lot of combat engineers... excellent performance" for blob support. **(2) We have been income-capped all campaign and the enemy has not**: every scorecard reads `no pool free of 9`, and the fabricator building (2,200cr, credits at a quarter of an extractor's rate, pool-free) is the community's answer to pool exhaustion. **(3) The one verified 1v1 Impossible win in the Steam thread used lava terrain as the wall** -- terrain-anchored defence again, consistent with the bridge findings. The scholarly sweep (OpenAlex, 67+38 works) confirmed the frame -- scripted RTS AIs are canonically exploitable by cheese the StarCraft competitions documented -- but nothing sharper than our own decompile. **The decoy verb shipped**: doctrine int `decoys`, scouts posted at flank fractions of the base-to-base line on OUR half, the lurker's flee cycle re-used, one scout-shortfall count shared by all three scout verbs so they never fight over a unit. `scatter` (decoys 5) and `legion` (mass 40 + combatEngineer in the heavies, inert-until-offered) probe Impossible now.

## [2026-07-31] measure | scatter and legion close the day's map: the lottery is real but thin, the healer is real but unaffordable
Artifacts: `runs/{scatter,legion}.out`
Notes: The two research-backed arms, both dead, both informative. **Scatter (decoys 5): defeated at 2,625** -- an 11% survival gain over baseline and the quietest early-game of any Impossible arm (71 attacks at mid-match where every prior arm stood at 100-400), which is what dilution looks like -- but five tickets among fifty units misdirects a tenth of the waves, and a tenth is not a mechanism at 3.7x. **Legion (mass 40 + combatEngineer in the heavies): wiped at 3,074, the day's longest-lived probe**, and its ledger answers the roster question the community raised: `produce:combatEngineer asked 181 got 0` -- **the tier-two land factory OFFERS the combat engineer**; the 3,500 price simply never fit a tick under pressure. The healing blob is reachable and unaffordable, the same shape as the siege piece. The day's Impossible ledger closes at roughly 0/70 with every planner-expressible lever measured, and the arc that remains is architectural: the reflex layer -- combat micro executed engine-side between samples, the planner setting posture, the agent playing the hands. Everything shipped today (heavies, walls, income posts, all-in timing, decoy geometry) is a doctrine knob waiting under those hands.

## [2026-07-31] build | the reflex layer: the bot grows hands
Artifacts: `agent/src/rwbot/agent/Reflexes.java`, `CommandRecord/CommandChannel` (posture verb + every-tick hook), `src/rw_bot/wire/command.py` (PostureOrder), `src/rw_bot/policy/dispatching.py` (send_postures), doctrine flags `kite`/`hp_floor`, `doctrines/reflex.doctrine`, gate green
Notes: The campaign's first architectural build, aimed by seventy Impossible refutations and the community meta they all pointed at: the rung is beaten by HANDS -- artillery kited perpetually outside turret reach, hurt units pulled out and back -- reactions below any sample interval. The agent already ran a self-reposting runnable on the game thread every engine tick (the lockstep hold); the reflex pass rides the same hook every five ticks, fifteen times the planner's cadence, and issues nothing but movement: **kite** (with a reach advantage over the nearest threat, step directly away whenever it closes inside its own reach plus a margin -- the engine fires automatically in range, so kiting is pure movement) and **flee-hurt** (below the doctrine's health floor with a threat in reach, walk out). The posture table rides a new flat wire verb, one row per profiled type, reach from the planner's own catalogue -- the agent never guesses a stat, and an unknown attacker reads as unarmed with the margin absorbing the error. The planner stays in command: a reflex never attacks, never spends, and the engine running the newest waypoint hands every unit back the moment the reflex goes quiet. Validation ladder: Hard probe (running), the VH champion regression (13/24-0 must not break), then Impossible.

## [2026-07-31] measure+build | the reflex A/B convicts both hands, the kite gets stutter-steps, and the bot finally reads the scoreboard
Artifacts: `runs/reflex-{kite,flee}.out`, `agent/src/rwbot/agent/Reflexes.java` (computed steps), `src/rw_bot/policy/situation.py`, doctrine ints `strike`, `doctrines/{hospital,striker}.doctrine`, gate green
Notes: The reflex A/B at Hard, bit-exact on one seed: baseline 28,500 worth; kite-only 26,200; flee-only 23,200; both 23,800. **Both hands lose as first built, for reasons the numbers name.** Flee's wound is structural: a fled unit with nowhere to heal is subtracted from every later fight -- the fix is the pairing the community plays, flee TOWARD a repair bay, shipped doctrine-only as the `hospital` arm (repairbay in the goals the flee runs home past). Kite's wound was arithmetic: most units cannot fire while moving, and a fixed 140-unit step put a 0.6-speed artillery piece into ~230 frames of silence per trigger -- the step is now COMPUTED, exactly out of the band plus a buffer, floor 30, which is what human stutter-step does between shots. Re-measuring.

**And the architecture question Austin asked got its first answer in code.** The engine broadcasts every player's army value and income unfogged in every sample; the bot has carried the figures all campaign and read them for one report line. `policy/situation.py` is the information layer -- pure scoreboard reads -- and its first consumer is the **strike window**: `strike N` releases the whole horde while our army value is at least N percent of the strongest rival's, replacing the all-in's clock and the riposte's intrusion edge with the signal both were approximating. The `striker` arm (mass 40, strike 80) probes Impossible now: hold everything, and go the moment their force is broken.

## [2026-07-31] measure | the reflex round at Hard, and the ratio window that cannot open at Impossible
Artifacts: `runs/{reflex-kite2,hospital,striker-imp}.out`
Notes: The round's table at Hard, bit-exact on one seed against the 28,500 baseline: computed-step kite 21,250 (WORSE than the fixed step -- smaller steps against faster chasers is a permanent shuffle that never fires, so the kite is now speed-gated: only a unit faster than its chaser kites, which is the hover unit's move and never the 0.6-speed artillery's); flee-to-hospital 24,650 (the repair bay buys back 1,450 of flee's 5,300 loss -- the loop works, underpowered at a rung where every fight is already favorable). The coherent reading: **at a winning rung, any reflex that leaves a fight trades away a win in progress; the reflexes' hypothesis lives where fights are unfavorable, and must be measured there.** And the striker at Impossible: wiped at 2,928 with zero marches, because `strike 80` is an absolute ratio and their army is ALWAYS three times ours at that rung -- the window that marks their broken waves there is MOMENTUM, their army value dropping twenty thousand as a wave dies on our line, a relative cross-tick signal the situation layer does not yet carry. Next session's spine, in order: momentum in `policy/situation.py` (previous-tick rival value, release on the drop), the reflex pair measured AT Impossible on the champion, and the combat-engineer funding that closes the heal loop the hospital arm proved.

## [2026-08-01] build+research | momentum ships, the medic channel ships, and the shallow-mechanics audit is on the record
Artifacts: `src/rw_bot/policy/{situation,medic}.py`, `doctrines/striker.doctrine` (strike 15000 = rival army-value drop), `wiki/pages/mechanics-healing.md`, wire-contract + issuing-orders pages refreshed, gate green
Notes: Austin's spine, executing in order. **Momentum**: `Momentum` tracks the strongest rival's army value over a forty-sample window and `strike N` now opens on a FALL of N credits from the recent peak -- the wave-died-on-our-line signal -- replacing the ratio one probe refuted. **The medic channel**: combat engineers hired one at a time through the withhold pattern (`medics N` keeps a headcount alive, one order outstanding, saving toward 3,500 when refused), closing flee-to-heal-to-rejoin structurally. Both at Impossible now (striker s12345, reflex pair s12345, parallel).

**The healing mechanics are investigated rather than assumed** (Austin's ask): decompiled `units/d/r` proves the repair bay heals AUTOMATICALLY -- radius 230, 0.2 per application, its own update timer, no order anywhere on the path -- and the combat engineer's auto-heal is marked inferred pending one hp-series probe, which is the honest tier ([[mechanics-healing]]). **The shallow-implementations audit**, on the record: (1) volley math ignores SHOOT DELAY -- a 150-frame artillery reload and a 40-frame tank reload count the same, so kill-group sizing flatters slow shooters; (2) SHIELDS are invisible to combat profiles (hp only) -- gunship and laser-shield units read as weaker than they fight; (3) fire-on-move is unmodeled -- the kite gate assumes stationary fire universally, and NamuWiki names units that fire moving; (4) the unit cap is read but never observed binding; (5) the engineer auto-heal above. Deep where it was suspected shallow: attack-move (decompile + live probe), the ability key path (six probes deep), the option stream (recaptured twice under contract). Wiki drift fixed the same pass: the wire-contract page's example block now shows the real current records (key, price, player rows), the verb roster section lists all seven, and the stale `action` field is gone from every example.

Artifacts: `src/rw_bot/policy/{defence,expander,budget,convert}.py`, `scripts/results.py` (+tests), doctrine fields `bunkers`/`flame` (RW-DOCTRINE-020/021), `doctrines/tech-flame.doctrine`, `runs/tech-cover-fund.out`, `runs/tech-flame.out`
Notes: The champion's ledger named its own defect (defence reached 954, acted 2) and the fix is a three-piece saving loop: expand_defence checks demand before price so a priced-out wait implies a bare structure (priced_out=True); the Expander carries the deficit cross-tick and withholds it EARLY (fund_cover), binding produce/upgrades/creep; Budget.release hands it back at the expander's own step where income and defence arbitrate in their measured order — income keeps absolute priority, the Hard refutation (29 turrets vs 4 extractors) stays honoured. Measured: defence asked 19 got 19 + 9 AA, "every structure already has cover" — the channel finished its job for the first time in the record. The flame Converter walks the turret fork upgradeable() skips (conversion price 700 by the engine's own option field), remembers holders (conversions never fill the queue — the extractor duplicate-crash precedent), withholds when refused. tech-flame (cover + funded defence + flame 2): **wiped at 3,938 samples, the longest survival ever, dip 6,200, 88 kills**. Results index shipped (scripts/results.py: per-probe table + long-form spend ledger, regenerated from runs/*.out so it cannot drift) — it immediately surfaced bridge-cover2's 7,750 dip as prior replication and forced difficulty/map columns. Paired-seed validation of tech-flame at 777/424242 launched.

## [2026-08-01] protocol | ad-hoc concurrency breaks same-seed determinism; flame-strike "refutation" retracted
Artifacts: `runs/flame-strike.out` vs `runs/tech-flame.out` (same seed 12345, same effective decisions), corrected entry in [[community-play-strategies]]
Notes: flame-strike's worst rival drop was 2,050 against a 5,000 strike threshold — `strike_window` never opened, the machinery never acted, and the run's decisions were plain tech-flame's. Yet it wiped at 2,253 where tech-flame reached 3,938 at the same seed. The difference in conditions: tech-flame ran beside a second engine, flame-strike solo. The sweep caveat (PARALLEL runs are not bit-identical) evidently extends to concurrent ad-hoc runs: same-seed comparisons are valid only under identical load, and single-run survival deltas of ~1,100 samples sit inside the noise band. Consequences applied: the flame-strike refutation is retracted (strike remains unmeasured — and structurally needs rush 1 to convert a release into a march); distribution-level claims (stack dips clustering 6-7k) stand, single-run deltas carry the band. Probes launched: flame-long (10,000 samples, does the wall hold indefinitely / does their army saturate at the unit cap) and flame-strike2 (strike 5000 + rush 1, the functional opportunistic release).

## [2026-08-01] measure | the imp-flame sweep: the stack's records were variance, retention gains are real, verdicts unchanged

Real and surviving: extractor retention 54 drops vs 71, median ending worth 350 vs 0, income alive at the end 3 matches vs 1 — the funded-defence and flame mechanisms work as built and buy measurable but marginal ground. Strategic state after ~120 Impossible matches: no doctrine knob, fortification, economy or composition change has altered a verdict; the 3.7x income handicap defines the game. The next frontier is not another arm off this chassis — it is either a fundamentally different win path (verified engine win/elimination conditions, or exploiting the AI's scripted behavior seams recorded in [[ai-opponent-strategy]]) or accepting the handicap and measuring down-ladder wins watchably per the project goal.

## [2026-08-03] measure+protocol | the verdict sweep and the coin-flip finding: worker count flips seeds
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/vh-{siege,verdict}/`, `sweeps/vh-{siege,verdict}.txt`
Notes: The merge (flame-siege = wall + retimed ladder) was refuted 0/6 -- credit-competition cannibalization, the third replication of winners-don't-stack. The retimed ladder's full-24 verdict came in 8W/15L -- and seeds 60613 and 264575, won by the SAME config in the 6-worker screen hours earlier, lost under 8 workers. With the Impossible-side evidence (byte-identical scorecards under matched sweep conditions; divergence under differing load), the picture is now complete: at the 10k horizon a VH match is a biased coin and concurrency perturbs the flip. Five sweeps of "seed-trading" were this. Consequence for the goal: 8-worker sweeps measure a noisier distribution than deployment (watchable = solo); the candidate's true figure is its SOLO win rate, now being measured (workers=1, sequential). Consequence for the method: distribution comparisons across sweeps remain valid at matched worker counts; per-seed checklists do not.

## [2026-08-05] code | the economy floor becomes the map's own answer: floor = reachable pools minus the rival's share
Artifacts: `src/rw_bot/policy/expander.py` (`economy_floor()`, `RIVAL_POOL_SHARE=2`, `FLOOR_MINIMUM=1`, latch on `Expander`), `src/rw_bot/policy/economy.py` (+`visible`/`unreachable` on `Expansion`), pins in `tests/test_campaign_loop.py`, `sweeps/run-floor.sh` (validation launched)
Notes: The literal ECONOMY_FLOOR=7 replaced by a runtime derivation from the survey the economy already runs: **floor = max(1, visible − unreachable − 2)**. The rival's share of 2 is measured, not chosen -- every winning vh-solo24 trace ends the race with 8-9 of duel_lake's 9 pools occupied and the bot holding 6-7, so seven was always "everything reachable minus what the Very Hard opponent lands" wearing one map's units (census strings in `runs/sweeps/vh-solo24`: "no pool free of 9"). The census rides the `Expansion` record (economy decisions carry it; factory/turret decisions never surveyed and are judged by the latched last answer); duel_lake derives exactly 7, hills_2's 8 visible derive 6, and a 3-pool map derives 1 so protection ends where the map's race does instead of where duel_lake's did. Pins hold both sides of the duel_lake boundary (7 owned yields to the reserve, 6 builds past it) and the cross-map fix (1 of 3 pools already yields). Validation running under matched conditions: the four xmap maps against their 0W/5L/3S baseline (seeds 12345/777, 2 workers) plus a duel_lake solo regression pair on two baseline wins (16180, 90210).

## [2026-08-05] code | the wire's eighth verb: ability_at, a key dispatch with the planner's own ground point
Artifacts: `src/rw_bot/wire/command.py` (TargetedAbilityOrder + shared key validator), `src/rw_bot/wire/posture.py` (posture split out -- command.py had crossed the 600-line cap), `src/rw_bot/control/channel.py` (send_targeted_ability), `agent/src/rwbot/agent/{CommandRecord,CommandChannel,Orders,WireChecks}.java` (ABILITY_AT parse/dispatch/execute + selftest rows), `scripts/nuke_probe.py` (+tests), pins in `tests/test_wire_command.py`/`test_control_channel.py`
Notes: The nuke launch is declared `fireTurretXAtGround: siloTop` -- the engine aims the silo at the point the command carries -- and the plain ability verb could never express it (it sends the unit's own position, a placeholder nothing reads on production actions). The extension is exactly the one the research predicted: `Orders.ability` already ended at `newPoint(unit position)`, so the targeted form is the same dispatch with the given point; the shared body was lifted, not forked. A verb of its own rather than an optional field, matching the wire's exact-shape rule (an ability that takes no point refuses one; one fired at the ground cannot omit it). Registry facts pinned from the assets: `nukeLauncherC` 45,000, placed by the ordinary `builder` (buildedge 28, techLevel 1, no gate); `buildNuke` 11,000 -> ammo, cap 4; `launchNuke` price ammo=1, 3.5s charge; warhead 123 direct + 5,400 area over radius 250 (`nuke_launcher.ini`). The cheap validator candidates all fell to the same tell -- missileAirship's ground strike and the modular spider's artillery are `[comment_action_...]`, commented out of this build -- so the live probe validates on the real unit: fund the launcher through the plan, stockpile, and land the strike on the probe's own farthest extractor, whose death at known coordinates is the observable. Probe running (`runs/nuke-probe.out`, easy opponent, seed 42). Tree note: the four pending floor-validation sweeps were pre-frozen before any of this landed, so the measurement chain still runs the floor code exactly as validated.

The floor derivation itself remains motivated by duel_lake's own trace evidence, not by the confounded runs.

Notes: The decompile named the accident exactly: `loadConfigCommon` reads `numberOfAIs` off the OPEN document with a Java fallback of FOUR, capped by the map's spawn count -- and every "(2p)"-named skirmish map except duel_lake carries four spawns. The fix is the engine's own script-callable setter: `setValueById('numberOfAIs', N)` written between the document open and the sandbox start, exactly where the GUI itself would have written it (the old edit-the-.rml failure is explained, not contradicted: the FILE carries no value attribute; the live document does). Chained: the champion across all four foreign maps as TRUE duels (`duel-<map>` sweeps), the cross-map question asked cleanly for the first time. 1vX seating (opponents=2,3) on bigger maps is now one job-spec token away -- the goal's "any configuration" axis is configurable.

## [2026-08-05] research | kill attribution is a field read: the engine keeps lastDamagedBy on every unit
Artifacts: `.decompiled/com/corrodinggames/rts/game/units/custom/logicBooleans/UnitReference$LastDamagedByUnitReference.java` (reads `y.bt`), task #32
Notes: Asked "what kills our units, what kills our buildings, what do we kill" -- currently unanswerable: deaths are inferred by roster disappearance and the report counts HOW MANY, never BY WHAT. The decompile answers with a gift: the modding system's `lastDamagedBy` boolean reads a unit field (`y.bt`) the engine maintains natively, so true attribution needs no combat-event patch -- the agent walks every unit every sample already and can cache the last damager's type, emitting a `death` record when the unit vanishes. Design filed as task #32 (death watch -> wire record -> scorekeeper tables -> autopsy columns). First customer: the erosion class -- "loses every extractor after winning the race" becomes a named enemy composition to counter, feeding the census (#28) and army tasking (#30). Also noted this session: replay determinism stands (bit-identical solo replays); the residual spread is chaos, not noise, and the unclaimed statistical headroom is a paired-seed reader, trace-metric-first judgment, and bigger overnight panels.

## [2026-08-05] research | the engine is not slow, it is SLEEPING: fast-forward is a public setter away
Artifacts: `.decompiled/com/corrodinggames/rts/java/b.java:123` (Display.sync(targetFPS)), `u.java:117,142-144` (setTargetFrameRate 300/120/30), task #35
Notes: "Why can't we make a gym mode" turned out to deserve a better answer than "we can't". Scorecard engine-clocks vs wall time show headless matches at ~1x realtime -- and the decompile names the reason: the Slick container sleeps to a 300fps target, which at the pinned 3ms logic step is exactly 0.9x realtime. The step pin (policy-determinism's fix) means raising the target frame rate produces MORE identical 3ms steps per wall second: same simulation, faster wall clock, verifiable bit-for-bit via the trace's world-digest column. The setter is public and the agent already holds the container (pinLogicStep). Task #35 carries the build: agentArgs knob, re-apply on stride (the engine re-sets its targets on state changes), harness plumbing after the running panel frees play.ps1, and a normal-vs-fast digest comparison as the acceptance gate. Honest ceilings recorded: CPU per tick and planner think-time bound the speedup (est. 3-5x per instance -- panels overnight -> ~2h, 96-seed panels routine); AlphaStar-scale RL stays out of reach and the tier-1/tier-2 model plan stands.

## [2026-08-06] research | fast-forward: three A/Bs and the drain that erases naive injections; the fix is a one-site bytecode patch
Artifacts: `runs/ff-{normal,fast,normal2,fast2,fast3}.{out,log}` + traces, `FastForward.java` + full knob plumbing (parser -> options -> MatchSetup -> play.ps1 -> Makefile, all green), task #35 (mechanism + design)
Notes: The gym question got the full treatment. (1) Free-running A/B: identical walls -- wrong experiment, sampling is wall-paced there. (2) Lockstep A/B with setTargetFrameRate 100000: 116.2s vs 115.8s -- the fixed-timestep accumulator adds MEASURED wall delta per pass, so game-time-per-wall-second is wall-locked by construction; the frame-rate target only spins the loop faster with proportionally smaller deltas. (3) Accumulator injection per tick, confirmed armed at "10x": 116.2s vs 116.2s -- the drain (b.java:81-97) computes its update quotient BEFORE the loop and OVERWRITES storedDelta with a fresh modulo afterward, erasing anything injected from inside game.update. Also settled: -nodisplay uses the same windowed container class (Main.java:481), just 10x10. The fix that survives the drain: patch the delta at its source -- rewrite the gameLoop's getDelta() call site to scale through a static hook in agent code (ClassFilePatcher precedent; javaagent jars are system-classpath-linkable). Scaled delta enters before the accumulator, so quotient and remainder stay consistent. Every dead end measured, every claim line-cited; the knob plumbing and debugged A/B protocol carry over unchanged. Baseline pinned: 116.2s / 400 samples / seed 9.

## [2026-08-05] design-update | match service goes Postgres-first, ML stays in covenant, runners are pluggable
Pages updated: harness-match-service (queue store, size budget, ML seam, multi-game section)
Notes: Austin's questions reshaped three decisions, all now on the page. (1) The queue is a Postgres table (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, heartbeat, lease table) instead of Redis+RQ -- one store, durable, job history as SQL rows; `platform_workers.job_store` is Redis-backed so the claim query is new-but-standard code. Results and logs land in the same DB: measured ~17MB raw per 24-panel (~686KB trace + 4.5KB card + 32KB logs per match), traces compress ~10x, so nightly panels for a year sit around 1GB with log pruning -- the GB worry is settled by arithmetic. runs/ stays canonical in phase 1, dual-written. (2) The match service never grows a GPU: simulation is CPU Java parallelized by leases; training stays in covenant-radar-api, whose GPU worker (RTX 3090 Ti) was verified end-to-end today -- the seam is the exported CSV, deliberately boring. (3) Job schema carries a runner discriminator; TankpitBot becomes an adapter behind the same queue later, with the caveat that its parallelism is bounded by live-server sessions, not CPU.

THE DECISIVE NEXT INSTRUMENT (one run): from the per-tick hook, count loop passes per wall second and update-calls per pass, and log the identity hash of the spoofed container against the one handed to game.update -- that answers "is the drain seeing 30ms" and "is our b THE b" simultaneously; then it is either an identity fix or the getDelta bytecode patch. ## [2026-08-06] research | fast-forward bottoms out: the sim ticks once per RENDER pass; the container drain is vestigial
Artifacts: `runs/ff-diag{,2,3,4,5}.log` (eight instrumented runs), `FastForward.java` (diag machinery: passes/s, live field state, executing stack), task #35 (true mechanism + two fixes)
Notes: Eight nulls resolved into one sentence: this engine advances its simulation once per render pass, clamped by its own delta override (`l.bu` -- the very field our determinism pin sets), and Slick's fixed-timestep drain does not drive it. The proof chain: identical walls across seven lever variants; the runtime stack showing our hook (and the sim's work) under `u.render:1661`; and the live state read `min=3 max=3 smooth=true fps=33 stored=2` -- every container-side input correct, the drain consuming ~3ms per pass regardless. The determinism page had it in writing all along ("a render-only cycle ticks the simulation"); the lesson is the same one the seating bug taught: the decompile's SHAPE is not the runtime's FLOW, and one stack trace was worth five theories. Fixes filed in preference order: (A) extra engine ticks per render pass from the agent's existing render-phase hook -- preserves the 3ms quantum, digest-identity testable; (B) scaling bu -- one line, but coarser physics, never bit-identical, needs statistical equivalence instead. The knob, baseline (116.2s/400), and diag instruments all carry forward.

## [2026-08-06] BREAKTHROUGH+caveat | fast-forward works: 3.3x measured wall speedup, bit-identity pending a quiet machine
Artifacts: `FastForward.java` (final form: boundary-aligned extra engine ticks), `runs/ff-real{,2}.{out,log,trace}`, task #35
Notes: Fix A landed. The accelerator invokes the engine's own tick entry (`l.a(float,int)`, the u.render:710 call) up to multiplier-1 extra times per render pass, never past the next lockstep sample boundary -- so accelerated runs sample the SAME frames realtime runs sample. Measured at the 10x knob: **34.9s vs the 116.2s baseline (3.3x)**, 1,110 frames/s vs ~294, identical frames elapsed, identical engine clock (89,775ms -- the clock derives from frames, not the passed ms), identical verdict AND final worth. Digest comparison: fast-vs-normal bit-identical for 293/400 samples before a content-level fork (suspect: a per-PASS random draw off the sim path -- pass counts differ by speed, shifting a generator sequence). The open caveat: fast-vs-fast replicated only 96/400 -- but every run tonight shared the machine with the ledger panel, the exact parallel-load condition the determinism arc proved breaks replay even at realtime. CERTIFICATION PROTOCOL (quiet machine, panel finished): normal-vs-normal identity first (re-establish the floor), fast-vs-fast second, fork-frame diagnosis third if needed -- the digest column turns any failure into a frame number. Speedup accounting at 10x: pass rate halves under the extra work (132/s), so 10 frames/pass x 132 = ~4.5x sim with ~1.2s planner overhead per 100 samples; higher knobs and planner trimming push toward the 6-8x ceiling. The knob rides every existing surface (PLAY_FASTFORWARD), inert at 0.

## [2026-08-06] research | the fork matrix: replication failure is one binary event at frame 7200, and the accelerator is exonerated
Artifacts: scratchpad fork-matrix over ten seed-9 traces (`runs/ff-{normal,normal2,fast,fast2,fast3,fast4,fast6,fast7,real,real2}.trace`), bytecode reads of `gameFramework/f` via javap against the pinned jar
Notes: Instead of waiting for a quiet machine, the ten traces already on disk were mined pairwise: matching-digest rows and first-fork frame for every pair. Three verdicts. (1) `ff-normal`+`ff-fast` differ from everything at frame 0 (older tree, different starting credits) -- excluded. (2) The remaining EIGHT same-config runs -- realtime, null-lever realtime, and both true 10x runs alike -- agree bit-for-bit to frame 7200 and then split into exactly TWO families ({normal2, fast7, real} vs {fast2, fast3, fast4, fast6, real2}) on one event: the rival's fifth unit appears in both, worth +600 in one family and +700 in the other. One AI build pick, two outcomes. Later intra-family forks (8625, 14925, 15825, 16650, 21975, 22050) are the same shape: discrete events, not drift. (3) **The accelerator is clean**: realtime-vs-realtime replicated 294/400 (fork@22050), fast-vs-realtime 293/400 (fork@21975) -- fast mode matches realtime exactly as well as realtime matches itself, so the replication failure is ambient, not the gym. Bytecode findings behind it: the engine's own per-match randomness reset (`f.a()`) seeds generator `b`, which NOTHING draws from; all five draw helpers read generator `a` (javap-verified) -- the engine seeds a generator it does not use. Our agent already compensates (EngineRandom pins `f.a` AND `Math.random` at premain and again at match start), which is why 7200 frames of agreement exist at all. Remaining unseeded suspects: `Collections.shuffle` on the AI's unit-mix rebuild (`game/a/d.java:43` -- a third stream neither pin reaches), per-call `new Random()` sites (`rts/a/a/*`, likely off the sim path), and draw-COUNT variance on the pinned streams. No wall-clock reads in AI or unit sim code (grep-verified). Instruments landed: `Collections.shuffle`'s generator is now seeded alongside the other two (EngineRandom, `--add-opens java.base/java.util` on all three launchers), and a per-sample RNG LEDGER (RandomLedger; `rng frame=N engine= math= shuffle=` in the agent log) states every generator beside every sample -- two runs' ledgers name the stream and the 75-frame window where draws desync, or prove no stream did. Replication A/B with the new pin is running; a loaded machine is now the RIGHT condition, because a correct pin must replicate under load.

## [2026-08-06] code | rw_matches registers covenant-side, and the split learns to keep matches whole
Artifacts: covenant_ml (registry entry `rw_matches` -- target `won`, group_column `match`, excludes arm/seed/verdict; `DatasetConfig.group_column`; `LoadedDataset.groups`; CSV loader factorizes the group column and never features it; parquet cache carries a groups sidecar; `stratified_split(groups=...)` splits whole groups stratified by group label), covenant_nn + all seven backends (pass-through), covenant-radar-api (worker feeds `dataset["groups"]` to the trainer; docs), tests everywhere
Notes: The load-bearing decision is that the registration was NOT just a registry row. The service's split was row-level and shuffled, and ~1,500 snapshots of one match are near-duplicates -- row-split rw_matches would score memorization as skill, which the #33 plan pinned from the start ("split by match, never by row"). So `groups` rides the LoadedDataset from CSV to cache to every backend's split call: one optional argument on the one split function all seven backends already share. ARFF and time-series loaders REFUSE grouped configs rather than silently dropping groups. `n_samples_expected=0` now legally means "grows with every export, shape contract only" -- the registry invariant test distinguishes it. Gates: covenant_ml 2243 tests / covenant_nn 161 / covenant-radar-api 2570, all at 100.00% statement+branch, ruff+guard+strict-mypy clean in all three. Operational lesson paid for tonight: running two 16-worker suites concurrently alongside the engine and the docker fleet starved the machine and crashed the session -- suites now run one at a time at `-n 4`. Remaining before first training run: mltrace6's traces (chained behind ledger-solo24), `export_matches`, then a train-external request with `dataset: rw_matches` against a RESTARTED api+worker (the running pair imported the pre-registration code).

## [2026-08-06] BREAKTHROUGH | determinism cracked: the particle thread shared the AI's random stream, and the split generator ends it
Artifacts: `RandomLedger.java` (per-sample generator states), `RandomTap.java` (per-caller draw counts, `PLAY_RNGTAP=1`), `SplitRandom.java` (the fix), `EngineRandom.swapEngineGenerator` (Unsafe swap + read-back verify), CommandChannel (probe-race latch fix), `runs/{rng-a2,rng-b,rt-a,rt-b,tap-a,tap-b,tap2-a,tap2-b,tap3-a}.{log,trace}`
Notes: The ledger chain, run to ground in one session. (1) Pinned pairs (all three streams seeded) still forked; the ledger showed the ENGINE stream's states desyncing by frame 150 while worlds stayed bit-identical for ~9,000 more frames -- a draw-count/order effect, not a value effect. (2) One pair sampled 0,75,... vs 1,76,...: a READINESS PROBE connecting and leaving released the match-start hold for exactly one tick before the real planner attached. Fixed in CommandChannel: until a planner has acked once, a departure re-enters the wait on the SAME frame; the world cannot move until the first sample is consumed. (3) The next aligned pair showed the impossible: identical per-window draw counts, boundary states that desync at 75 and RE-CONVERGE at 150 -- the signature of a SECOND THREAD drawing from the shared generator (boundary reads race the drawer; totals converge; which thread gets which value flips per run). Both realtime pairs forked at the same frame (9525) with different outcomes: fork position set by the game, outcome by the scheduler. (4) The fixed tap (per-caller + per-thread counting; first install was silently overwritten by the holder's <clinit> when armed at premain -- swaps now verify by read-back and install at match start) named it in one window: `Thread-2@gameFramework.d.c.a:1501` -- THE PARTICLE SYSTEM spawns effects from a background thread and draws cosmetic jitter from the SAME generator the opponent AI's build/target decisions use, even headless. (5) The fix is SplitRandom, installed at match start on every seeded match: draws from the game thread (captured at install) come from the seeded stream; every other thread draws a salted side stream. Sim determinism becomes a pure function of the seed regardless of scheduling; effects lose nothing (their draws were never reproducible). No bytecode patch needed -- the engine's own field carries the split object. Certification pair running. Panel caveat recorded: ledger-solo24's matches launched after ~01:30 carry the shuffle pin (harness boots from the working tree); tolerable for a killer-table panel, not a paired A/B.

## [2026-08-06] CERTIFIED | bit-exact determinism at both speeds: 400/400 digests, all streams identical, fast == realtime
Artifacts: `runs/cert7-{a,b}.{log,trace}` (realtime pair), `runs/certf-{a,b}.{log,trace}` (10x pair), `SplitRandom.java` (tick-split routing), `AiTimers.java`, `Orders.onEngineTick`, MatchSetup (pre-tick watcher), tasks #35+#36
Notes: The certification: realtime-vs-realtime 400/400 identical world digests; fast-vs-fast at 10x 400/400; **fast-vs-REALTIME 400/400** -- the accelerated simulation is bit-exact against the wall-clock one; engine/math/shuffle generator states identical at all 401 samples in every pair; measured on a machine concurrently running the ledger panel. Same seed now means same match, at either speed, under load. The residual leaks after the ambient silencer, in the order the composed tap (counts only sim-routed draws) named them: (1) the unit sway redraws (`units/e/b.a:260,265`) draw wall-valued deltas INSIDE the tick, handled by the tick-split generator -- SplitRandom routes each draw by STACK, not thread (everything is one thread): through `game.i.a(F)V`/`a(FI)V`/`b(FI)V` by descriptor = the seeded sim stream, reaching `java.u` first = a salted side stream; descriptors matter because `i.a(m.l,float)` is the world DRAW pass and a name-only match handed cosmetics the sim stream back. (2) The opponents' think-timer floats (`aP += f2` vs a 25-unit boundary, staggered per seat by `av()`) started each run polluted by the free ticks between world creation and the drain-time hold -- free ticks that carry the MEASURED delta because the pin lands at liveness. Compensations (timer reset via the AI's own `av()` + zeroing) helped but the structural fix ended it: the match watcher now rides the engine's PRE-update runnable queue (`game.i.k`, drained at the top of the guarded tick body, before the world updates -- and the engine object is a stable singleton, so the queue survives the start script). The watcher, pins, reseed, frame-zero, and hold all land BEFORE the new world's first update: zero free ticks by construction. Failed detours recorded for the next reader: swap-at-premain overwritten by `<clinit>` (verify swaps by read-back); thread-split routed nothing (single-threaded engine); `l.ar` is not the pause; a schedule-time delta pin targets machinery the start script replaces. The gym is now measurement-grade: fast panels are bit-exact replays of realtime panels at ~3.3x wall speed at the 10x knob.

(3) The death ledgers name the real difference: under the new regime the FIRST loss of s1337 is a builder to a c_tank at sample 630 followed by extractors picked off one by one -- economy harass -- where the old lineage's first losses are combat units trading at the front. The think-timer fix removed the free-tick pollution that had been DELAYING enemy AI aggression in every prior panel: the fork at s95 is the regime change, and 0/24 is the champion's true rate against a punctual Very Hard, not a software fault. Consequences: every historical win-rate (vh-final 14W/8L, the closer's ~70-80%) was measured against a handicapped opponent and must be re-earned under exact timing; old-lineage traces are cross-regime and not comparable; the deleted rw_matches export stays deleted for LABEL reasons only (single-class), not poisoning. The freeze-verify-go lesson for the match service survives with its example corrected: the smoke gate would have PASSED this tree, and rightly so.

## [2026-08-06] rootcause+fix | the ladder collapse was a compile-time poisoning: the plan read the BOOT SANDBOX as its opening roster
Artifacts: `scripts/play.py` (sandbox-swap wait in the settle, FRESH_ROSTER_MAX=4, SANDBOX_SWAP_SAMPLES=8, `owned at compile` now printed), `runs/sweeps/fix-probe-med/` (2 won + 1 survived, plan 9/9, armies 23-35 -- against 0/3 all-wiped this morning), trace `plan` column (Tick 16th, the instrument that found it), tests (sandbox-swap pair in test_play), all gates green
Notes: The "exact-timing regime collapses the ladder" verdict is RETRACTED in its causal claim. The chain, run to ground with the new per-sample plan column: the plan showed healthy s0-293, then blocked THE SAMPLE the third extractor completed -- entry 4 (c_tank) had no producer and no landFactory had been inserted at compile. `owned at compile (frame 0)` printed the reason outright: airFactory x2, gunships, turrets, an experimentalTank, landFactory x2 -- the ENGINE'S BOOT SANDBOX roster (the ten-player default world), served to the settle loop before the configured match went live, its frame counter already pinned to zero by the certified frame-zero machinery. Expansion saw a c_tank producer as owned, inserted nothing, and the 8-entry plan was dead at its first combat entry in every match of every panel -- which is what produced 0/24 VH, 0/24 Hard, 0/3 Medium, and the goals-reordered variant's 0/3 alike. The death-spiral and aa-cover-thrash observations stand as accurate descriptions of what a factory-less bot looks like; they were symptoms, not causes. Fix: when the opening roster is sandbox-rich (> FRESH_ROSTER_MAX complete), wait up to SANDBOX_SWAP_SAMPLES for the wholesale world swap (a roster sharing no unit id); a world that never swaps is a default-map probe's real world and compiles as before. The compile's input is now PRINTED in every run log, because this hid precisely as long as nothing recorded it. Ladder re-measurement under exact timing starts now: hard24b, then vh24b, at SWEEP_FASTFORWARD=10.

## [2026-08-06] milestone | the first win-probability model trains on real matches, end to end
Artifacts: covenant job 24249609 (dataset rw_matches, 295,229 samples / 51 matches / 16 features, ClearGBM, split BY MATCH), test AUC 0.790 / accuracy 0.755 on held-out matches, model active at covenant's `models/active_cgbm.json`; panels `mltrace-hard24b` (18W/4S/2L) and `mltrace24b` (8W/2S/14L) under exact timing
Notes: The full chain ran today for the first time: engine frame -> income-bearing trace -> `export_matches` -> grouped split -> ClearGBM -> live win-probability model behind `/ml/train-external`. The exact-timing TRUE baselines: Hard 18/24 (above the handicapped-era 14), Very Hard 8/24 -- the ladder is re-founded on honest numbers. Val AUC 0.68 vs test 0.79 is match-level variance with ~8 matches per eval split; more panels tighten it, and every future panel is one 10x sweep away. Ops lesson worth its line: the training was nearly poisoned by a STALE API -- hypercorn's spawn children survive their parent's kill and keep serving OLD CODE through inherited sockets (netstat attributes the listener to the dead parent; the children carry no identifying cmdline). Two nights of piped background launches had left three such orphans racing on one port. Services now launch with file redirection, never `| tail`, and a restart is verified by killing the spawn children, not the parent.

## [2026-08-06] correction | the archived wire capture carries the damaged_by FIELD, not 48 attributions
Artifacts: `wiki/sources/m6-wire/world-sample.ndjson` (frames 2703/2991/3289, engine clock 9110-11093ms), `Makefile` `WIRE_CAPTURE_AT ?= 16,17,18`, `runs/traces/vh-nocover24/` killer columns, `tests/test_campaign_loop.py`
Notes: The death-ledger entry above says the re-taken capture "now carries 48 live attributions". It carries 48 entity records carrying the FIELD, every value empty -- and it always will, because `wire-capture` samples three consecutive offsets at 16/17/18 WALL seconds and exits, which lands at 9-11 seconds of GAME time: 5 command centres, 5-8 builders, 4 extractorT1 still `complete:false` and the editor pseudo-unit per frame, no combat unit of any type on the map, and every one of the 48 records at full health (`hp == max_hp`, zero exceptions). The empty string is the correct reading of an undamaged world, not a failed field read -- the distinction worth writing down, because a silent `fieldIfPresent` miss would look identical in the file. Attribution is confirmed working from two other directions instead: `runs/traces/vh-nocover24/`'s killer column carries real attributions (c_tank 40, scout 33, lightGunship 18, commandCenter 18, hoverTank 12, ... plasmaTank 1 -- with the erosion signature, extractorT1 killed by scout, landing exactly where the panel analysis said it would), and the campaign e2e round-trips a non-empty `damaged_by` through the PRODUCTION encoder (`lines()` delegates to `encode_sample`) into the report's killer tables. The capture stays as it is: its job is the record SHAPE the decoder tests read, and `test_the_real_capture_carries_the_documented_roster`, `..._advances_at_the_measured_frame_rate` and `..._shows_credits_accruing` all pin this specific early world, so a capture late enough to hold a kill would re-pin three tests and cover nothing the campaign test already covers. The lesson is the small one this log keeps re-learning at larger scales: "the field is on the wire" and "the field has values on the wire" are different claims, and the second one needed a grep before it was written.

## [2026-08-06] harness | the match fleet — parallel matches with a control page, stdlib only
The RustedWarfare side of the two-game fleet ruling ("i want to ensure we can easily run multiple bots on the desktop. with a simple ui, easy for me or the ai to use"). `make fleet` runs `rw-fleet` (`harness/fleet.py` + `fleet_http.py` + `fleet_page.py`) on http://127.0.0.1:27500/: one user-owned manager process whose children are ordinary `make play` invocations, so every launch knob the Makefile owns — seed, map, opponents, difficulty, **fast-forward multiplier**, frozen tree — is a spawn parameter and the plumbing is never duplicated. PLAY_PORT's per-invocation randomization (27600-27999) is what makes the parallelism safe. Per-instance artifacts under `runs/fleet/`: the child transcript (`<i>.out`, where the planner's match report lands — the stats source; the page shows the verdict live and the full report on click) and the game log (`<i>.log`). Stop KILLS the process tree (`taskkill /T`) — a headless sim owes no teardown, unlike the tankpit fleet's sentinel stop; restart replays the exact spawn. No web framework: single-threaded `http.server` (this package imports no threading — see the coverage note), one pure router function, requests parsed by `ndjson.parse_object` and responses rendered by its new emit-side sibling `render_json` — typed end to end, no `json.loads` `Any` anywhere. Shared-guard amendment (own commit): `ExceptionsRule` now recognises `.write_line(` as a surfacing call — it IS this package's output channel, and the rule's regexes only knew logger spellings. Gate: guard 0, 100.00% coverage (1,183 tests), architecture wiring test extended with the `rw-fleet` entry point.

## [2026-08-06] instrument+verdict | grouped k-fold CV lands, and the model question closes measured
Artifacts: `services/covenant-radar-api/scripts/cv_external.py` (+tests, all gates), CV runs on rw_matches (438,619 rows / 75 matches / 5 folds / seed 42)
Notes: The single-split AUC had swung 0.79 -> 0.63 between exports for no modelling reason -- ~11 test matches per split is a dice roll. The fix existed since the AMEX work (`covenant_ml.validation.group_stratified_kfold_split`, whole groups per fold, stratified by group label); the new runner points it at any registered grouped dataset, with the inner early-stopping split grouped too. First readings: **ClearGBM 0.778 +/- 0.062, LightGBM 0.768 +/- 0.062** -- same spread, same fold ordering (both peak fold 1, trough fold 4). Three questions close at once: the model's honest strength is ~0.78 (both earlier single-split numbers were draws from this spread); ClearGBM >= LightGBM on this problem, so the house engine keeps its seat with data behind it; and the ensemble question dies on correlated errors -- a blend of models that make the same mistakes buys nothing. The evaluation protocol is deliberately fixed-hyperparameter: the script measures datasets and backends, not tuning. Next AUC gains come from matches (every panel adds 24 groups) and features, not from modelling ceremony.

## [2026-08-06] rootcause+fix | the seating anomaly was a missing map: stale clones, a silent fallback, and the xmap family voided
Artifacts: `runs/seat-probe4.log`/`seat-probe5.{log,ndjson}` (the repro), engine log lines `FileLoader: Could not find asset ... [p2]lake_2p.tmx` -> `Error loading map` -> boot sandbox, dir diff (`.game` 109 maps vs every clone 103 -- big_island, dire_straight, fire_bridge, hills_2p, lake_2p, two_cold_sides missing from all of `.game-w1..w3`), `runner.prepare_clone` `_sync_maps` fix (+2 tests), `runs/seat-evidence/` (quarantined w1 autosaves, ruled out)
Notes: Run to ground in five probes after standing three days as "some per-dir state nobody wrote". The chain: the six true-1v1 maps were added to the PINNED copy after the worker clones were made; `prepare_clone` reuses a clone after checking only REQUIRED_ENTRIES, so the clones never got them; the agent's start script ran, the engine failed the map load WITH AN ALERT NOTHING READ, and fell back to its boot sandbox -- a 3-to-5-player FFA whose seat count varies with the background-map rotation. That fallback is the whole anomaly: "numberOfAIs read 2" because the override wrote 2; three seated because the sandbox owns the seats. CONSEQUENCE, verified across every card: the xmap-2* batches and duel-lake_2p all read `players 4 -> N` with sandbox openings (worth 37,800, army 12 at s0) -- **the cross-map true-1v1 validation family never played its named maps and its conclusions are void.** Fix: `_sync_maps` in prepare_clone copies maps the pinned dir has and a clone lacks, reported in the launch log, never silent. Follow-up for the agent (queued): a configured match whose map fails to load must KILL the run, not drift into the sandbox -- the alert is right there in the log the whole time. The players line, `owned at compile`, and the fresh-start worth are the reader-side tells, and all 99 matches of today's five panels pass them.

Notes: The log carried the root cause; the pages now do too, each at the section a future reader would consult first. Cross-links tie the three tellings to one incident. fact_checked untouched on determinism and parallel-matches -- only the amended sections were verified, against today's probes and the dir diff.

## [2026-08-06] fix+instrument | the wrong-world guard: no world but the requested one can be measured
Artifacts: `agent/src/rwbot/agent/WrongWorldGuard.java` (+`MatchSetup` gate, `HostSetup` split, two new pinned names in `EngineNames`/`BindingCheck`), probes `runs/guard-negative{,2}.log`, `runs/guard-positive{,2,3}.log`, `runs/guard-bisect-noarm.log`
Notes: The queued follow-up to the seating anomaly, landed agent-side in three pieces. (1) The match watcher's liveness predicate gains a MAP TERM -- `l.dl` must equal the requested path before any world can receive the setup or open the channel, so difficulty, reseed, frame-zero and the hold land on the requested world BY CONSTRUCTION. (2) The engine's own automated-testing switch (`l.aT`, three readers total, engine-verified) is armed before the start: a load that fails crashes at its origin with the map named in the stack trace instead of raising an alert nothing headless reads. (3) A requested world that never arrives halts the JVM at 60s (exit 70, under the harness's 90s port wait so the agent's diagnosis wins the log race). Building it surfaced two engine facts the hard way. First: the menu background is a RUNNING MISSION DEMO whose local player owns units whenever the script phase says so -- menu1 and menu3 both pass the old player-and-units liveness predicate, and the start script executes a full frame after the runnable that queues it (the ScriptEngine drain snapshots before running), so the old watcher's first check RACED the load against a menu world that could latch -- the boot-sandbox compile poisoning was this race lost, found from the other end. Second: the engine's pre-tick queue drains with `while (peek) poll().run()`, so a check that reposts into its own drain SPINS THE GAME THREAD FOREVER -- measured as a run whose log simply stops at "match starting" (the bisect probe), which is why the gated watcher reposts through the script queue (snapshot semantics force the one-frame delay) and back onto the pre-tick queue, keeping the latch ahead of the new world's first update and the zero-free-ticks property intact. Verified in both directions: the real duel plays through untouched (planner, trace, scorecard normal), and a missing map dies loudly twice over -- the armed crash names the file, the deadline names what was live. The 60s deadline also cuts sweep losses: a wrong-world job used to idle until the harness's timeout; now it dies in a minute with the cause in its log.

## [2026-08-07] rootcause | the old latch always fired on the menu world, and three artifacts prove it
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/mltrace24/logs/flame-close-s12345.log` (poisoned) vs `runs/sweeps/mltrace24b/logs/` (healthy) -- identical latch signatures; frame-0 income ratios across `runs/traces/{mltrace24,mltrace24b,mltrace-hard24b,vh-nocover24,guard-smoke}` (1.78/1.39, exact multipliers everywhere)
Notes: The open question from the wrong-world guard ("why did the old code neither hang nor visibly poison every run") is closed, measured, and the answer rewrites the poisoning's mechanism. (1) EVERY pre-guard run latched on the menu world: poisoned and healthy logs alike show the latch ~5 lines after "match starting", AiTimers resetting "10 team(s)", and the planner-connect map scan reading the menu's 10 pools before a later scan reads duel_lake's 9. The old first check never failed -- which is why the same-drain repost spin sat unreachable until the map gate made a first check fail for the first time. (2) The setup surviving this was two lucky mechanisms, both now measured: difficulty stuck because the reflective write lands before open() and the sandboxOptions document round-trips current settings back through loadConfigCommon's "stomp" (frame-0 rival/bot income reads exactly 1.8x at VH and 1.4x at Hard in every batch including the poisoned one -- so the panels' rungs were always what they claimed); and the load ran UNDER the hold, deterministically, off the just-seeded generator. (3) The poisoning was therefore never an agent-side race lost: agent behavior was identical in poisoned and healthy batches, and the only difference was play.py compiling before vs after the sandbox swap. The compile fix (FRESH_ROSTER_MAX/SANDBOX_SWAP_SAMPLES) was the real fix for the real defect; the guard now retires the whole class by construction -- the latch cannot fire off the requested world, so the swap the planner used to wait out no longer exists as an observable state. Corollary recorded: the new latch reseeds AFTER the load, so "same seed = same match" now holds as documented rather than as "same seed = same load-residue"; pre-guard seeds will not reproduce bit-exact under the new agent, which frozen trees already express.

## [2026-08-07] rootcause+fix | cross-invocation determinism: the stack walk was the fourth source, and the tick bracket retires it
Artifacts: `agent/src/rwbot/agent/TickBracket.java` (new), `SplitRandom.java` (walk deleted, routes by phase), `FastForward.java` (extras bracketed); the hunt: `runs/traces/guard-certify{2..8}`, `runs/div-hunt*`, `runs/tap-hunt*`, `runs/rt-hunt*`; certification `runs/bracket-{ff1,ff2,rt1,rt2}-trace.ndjson`
Notes: Austin's push ("code doesn't guess, it's algorithmic") ran a defect to ground that yesterday's 400/400 certification could not see, because it only ever compared runs WITHIN one batch. Same-seed runs from SEPARATE invocations forked -- always in the opponent AI's behavior (an extra anti-air turret, different factory queues), always surfacing at a consequential roll (frame 7050 on duel_lake, realtime and 10x alike) -- while parallel replicas stayed bit-exact for whole panels. The hunt disproved, with artifacts: the load-drawn synced seed `bJ` (real hole, pinned from the match seed at latch -- but cross-pairs A==D with different bJ killed it as THE cause), entity ids (identical), menu rotation (all menu3), per-dir state (prefs byte-identical, mod-info cache inert), identity hashes (`-XX:hashCode=2` armed and repH still forked; flag removed as unjustified), and the draw-tap's own per-window counts (wall-cut windows over IDENTICAL worlds -- all three tapped runs matched bit-exact while their tallies wobbled; the per-window diff methodology is unsound at fast-forward). What survived every test: the split generator's per-draw StackWalker classification -- the one seam where a per-process input (the JIT-shaped frame stream) reached the sim path. One draw classified differently shifts the sim stream; the world forks at the next behavioral roll; twin processes share a JIT timeline, which is exactly why within-batch certification always passed. The fix routes by PHASE instead: `TickBracket` raises a flag at the top of each tick (pre-tick queue) and lowers it after the sim (script queue) -- two queue rides whose ordering the engine guarantees, self-sustaining from the latch, fast-forward extras bracketed explicitly, no walk, no per-draw cost. Certified: separate invocations bit-exact at 10x (250 samples) and realtime (150), and 10x-vs-realtime bit-exact (150). "A pure function of the seed" is now true in the direction it was never tested. Regime note: the bracket changes draw routing, so pre-bracket seeds do not reproduce under it -- batches state their regime by frozen tree, and every panel to date remains valid as the within-batch experiment it was.

## [2026-08-07] verdict | the cross-map family, honestly played at last: 3W/3S/2L, and one law explains all eight
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/xmap3-{lake_2p,big_island,hills_2p,two_cold_sides}/` (8 cards, every match line verified: true map, difficulty 2, real-duel compile), `runs/spawn-probe{1..3}-state.ndjson`, `runs/lake-probe-trace.ndjson`, `sweeps/xmap3-jobs.txt`
Notes: The family the seating anomaly voided, rerun on the guarded, bracketed stack -- flame-nocover, two seeds per map, VH, 10x. big_island 2W (9-10 extractors, 9/9 plans, closed). two_cold_sides 2 survived-at-cap (4 and 7 extractors, 9/9 plans -- holds but never closes). hills_2p split: s777 won at 8 extractors, s12345 died strangled at 1. lake_2p 0/2 (defeated + wiped): ZERO extractors ever stood in 20,000 samples -- the probes show the buy granted at s0 and a builder walking forever, with the RIVAL's income also flat at base the whole match: the map's pools are effectively unreachable from spawn for both sides, so the match degenerates into a base-income grind that the AI's flat 1.8x multiplier decides. One law covers all eight cards: **the verdict tracks whether the extractor economy physically ran.** The doctrine's combat generalizes; its opening assumes land-reachable pools and has no fallback where that is false -- the first genuinely map-conditional lever the ladder has surfaced (a water/starvation opening, or an early-all-in trigger on maps where both sides stay poor: time compounds the AI's multiplier there). Two diagnostic corrections recorded on the way to reading these cards honestly: the scorecard's `plan` line is the TERMINAL state, not the compile (compile succeeded on every map, live-probed), and spawn assignment did NOT vary across invocations (three probes, identical seats) -- the spawn-lottery theory died the same day it was born. What this round proves about the harness: every card names its map, wrong worlds are structurally impossible, and both losses are real gameplay -- the voided family's conclusions were about nothing; these are about the maps.

## [2026-08-07] correction | the xmap3 verdict misread its own cards: peaks, not endpoints, and the law is EROSION
Artifacts: `runs/traces/xmap3-*/flame-nocover-s{12345,777}.ndjson` (per-sample extractor and rival-income columns, all eight matches)
Notes: The family entry above claims lake_2p is a starvation map where "zero extractors ever stood" and "the rival's income stayed flat at base" -- both false, and false in exactly the way [[policy-trace]] warns about: they were read off the scorecard's `extractors 0 -> 0` ENDPOINTS and a 60-sample probe window extrapolated to a full match. The traces say: lake_2p peaked at 6 and 7 extractors before losing every one, with rival income compounding to 202/s and 187/s; hills_2p s12345 peaked at 7 before eroding to 1; every match in the family compiled its plan and ran its economy (peaks 6-10 across all eight cards). The corrected law: the verdict tracks whether the economy was HELD, not whether it ran -- winners and cap-survivors end at their peak, losers erode to nothing while the rival compounds. Which is the duel_lake erosion law, travelling: xmap3 is cross-map CONFIRMATION of the known VH weakness, not a new opening/terrain gap, and the map-conditional-opening lever proposed in the family entry is withdrawn. Expansion speed and the closer threshold stay the levers. What stands from the family entry: all eight match lines verified true, all compiles clean, big_island 2W, two_cold_sides 2 survived, the harness vindication, and the two diagnostic corrections. Found because Austin said "double check please" -- the check was rereading the traces the harness had already written.

## [2026-08-07] verdict | both named VH levers measured, both negative: the champion holds, and the levers were descriptions
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/vh-close2/` (1W/0S/23L; paired 1 up / 17 down vs vh-nocover24), `runs/sweeps/vh-workers10/` (8W/4S/12L; paired 4 up / 9 down), `doctrines/vh-close2.doctrine`, `doctrines/vh-workers10.doctrine`, same 24 seeds as the baseline, first panels run under certified cross-invocation determinism
Notes: The erosion analysis named two levers -- expansion speed and the closer threshold -- and the one-knob arms falsify both directions. close2 (latch at 2x instead of 3x sustained) is the worst arm on record: net -16, four former wins wiped, the premature-all-in mode the 3x-sustained latch was DESIGNED against, re-created at panel scale exactly as the doctrine docstring predicted (9 won / 13 lost when latching flickered, log 2026-08-01 -- the mechanism generalizes). workers10 (10 builders instead of 8) is net -5: three former wins to wiped, the extra ~1,000 early credits and build attention taken from the army exactly when the AI's first waves land. The corrected reading: "winners reach 6 extractors by s1500 and close early" is a DESCRIPTION of winning trajectories, not a pair of knobs -- both naive operationalizations backfire because the discriminator lives upstream, in the early skirmishes that decide whether expansion is affordable and dominance is real. Next instrument, not next arm: the baseline panel's own 12-vs-12 split already holds the answer -- diff the winning and losing seeds' first 1,500 frames (army composition, first-contact outcomes, extractor timing RELATIVE to skirmish results) before spending another panel on a guess. Method note: with the bracket regime, each arm's 24 matches are exact controlled experiments -- the paired movement is signal, not noise re-rolled.

## [2026-08-07] analysis | the 12-vs-12 split named the real discriminator: the RIVAL's income at s1500, not our expansion
Artifacts: `runs/traces/vh-nocover24/` (all 24 traces, group means at s500/s1000/s1500 and per-seed table), the two negative arms above as the falsification that forced this look
Notes: With both trajectory-reading levers measured negative, the baseline panel's own traces were made to confess. Group means: at s500 and s1000 the three verdict groups are near-IDENTICAL on every own-side figure -- extractors (4 then ~6), income, army, losses. Our expansion speed never discriminated anything, which is why workers10 could only hurt. The split opens between s1000 and s1500 on the OTHER side of the map: losers' rivals reach 82/s mean income at s1500 (winners' 64), and losers begin losing their own extractors in the same window. Per-seed it is a strong gradient rather than a law: rivals held to 54/s -> 7W/3S/1L; rivals reaching 83+ -> four of the seven losses, with s555 the lone win against a 97-income rival. The matches are decided s1000-1500 by WHOSE HARASSMENT LANDS -- suppressing the rival's expansion while surviving its first waves -- upstream of everything the erosion analysis described. The evidence-driven arm this names: the raid party, the one champion subsystem aimed at rival extractors, whose v2 verdict explicitly left party size as the open question ("at the first-wave size the raid is free and wins nothing", [[policy-raid]]) -- and which the AI research pass showed also leashes the AI's armies home via the 500-tick base-defence recall. vh-raid5 (raid 3 -> 5, all else champion) is running as this is written, same 24 seeds, paired.

## [2026-08-07] analysis | the VH wall has a name: the AI's naval branch, and it is air-blind
Artifacts: per-seed killer tables from `runs/traces/vh-nocover24/` (battleship kills vs verdict), `wiki/sources/m11-pools/type-flags.ndjson` combat profiles, `doctrines/vh-helo.doctrine` (the arm this names)
Notes: The 12-vs-12 split's rival-income discriminator resolves one level deeper in the killer tables: EVERY baseline loss carries battleship kills (6-44 per match) while eight of twelve clean wins carry zero -- the champion beats land AIs and loses to the naval branch, whose water-side expansion is what the s1500 rival-income figures were measuring (land raids cannot touch water extractors, and the fleet shells the shore). The profiles then hand over the counter mechanically: battleShip (range 240) and gunBoat (120) both carry hits_air=false -- the AI's observed navy core CANNOT SHOOT AIR -- its AA escorts (heavyAAShip, missileShip) appear in zero killer tables, and its scout (110, the champion's top harass killer) is OUTRANGED by c_helicopter (130, hits both layers). c_artillery (290) outranges the whole surface navy and is untouchable by AA ships, which hit air only. The counter system tilts only within types the doctrine names ("no unit is invented"), so the gap is a composition fact with a one-slot fix: vh-helo swaps the goals' hoverTank for c_helicopter, giving the tilt an air type to repeat at navies. Queued behind the running raid5 arm and the first honest Impossible probe (imp-nocover12, difficulty 3, 12 seeds -- the pre-regime 0/47 has never been re-measured). Tonight's pipeline: raid5 -> impossible12 -> helo, all on the certified bracket regime, all paired on the baseline seeds.

The champion's mix is razor-tuned; only strict addition remains untried, and vh-heloplus (heavies heavyTank,heavyTank,c_helicopter -- both tanks kept, the helicopter appended third) is chained behind the fortress screen as the night's last arm.
