## [2026-08-07] verdict | strict addition falsifies the last composition channel: eight arms, eight negatives, one law
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/vh-heloplus/` (24 cards: 8W/4S/12L; navy 4 up/5 down, land 0 up/6 down vs vh-nocover24)
Notes: The append arm closes the composition ladder: heavies heavyTank,heavyTank,c_helicopter -- both tanks kept, the helicopter strictly added -- still lands net -7, and still breaks land seeds, because production is RATIO-driven: every entry reweights the simplex, and there is no such thing as addition to a razor-tuned mix. Eight one-knob arms now stand measured negative (close2, workers10, raid5, helo, heloadd, heloheavy, heloplus, plus the fortress screen at Impossible), and one law covers them: any PERMANENT reshaping of the champion's opening or mix costs the land game more than it pays anywhere, while helicopters demonstrably lift naval stalemates whenever present. The conclusion was never "helicopters don't work" -- it is that the answer must be CONDITIONAL, spent only when the threat is seen. That is code, not a knob, and it landed tonight: the counter tilt's naval clause (counter.py, all gates green) repeats the doctrine's own fleet-outranging types -- the 290-range artillery against 240-range battleships -- only when WATER-layer movers are in the remembered picture. vh-navtilt24 measures it now: champion doctrine untouched, the tilt code the only delta, the eight-arm graveyard the bar it has to clear.

## [2026-08-07] verdict | the conditional naval tilt is the first non-negative arm in nine: a wash with a mechanism
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/vh-navtilt24/` (24 cards: 10W/3S/11L; paired 6 up / 8 down / 10 unchanged vs vh-nocover24), `src/rw_bot/policy/counter.py` naval clause + tests
Notes: Net -2 -- inside the wash band, where eight composition arms landed -5 to -16 -- and the per-seed table separates direction from magnitude cleanly. Every one of the six upgrades is a naval rescue (s42424 wiped->WON, s244948 and s60613 survived->won, two defeats to survivals): where the baseline lost to fleets, the tilt wins. Every one of the eight downgrades is a seed where the baseline was winning WITH ships in view: the tilt fired there too, and the 1:1 share arithmetic let a parked fleet drown the mix in artillery, displacing the land army that was already winning. Direction proven, magnitude uncalibrated -- the follow-up is a bounded tilt (cap the answering share) rather than a new idea, the first time since cover-off that an arm's failure mode is a CONSTANT and not a concept. The champion remains flame-nocover; the tilt code stays in the tree (it changes nothing when no fleet is seen, and the cap arm builds on it).

## [2026-08-07] correction | the navtilt downs are chaos re-rolls, not over-tilt: the cap premise dies on its own tables
Artifacts: loss-composition comparison baseline-vs-navtilt on s16180, s8128, s42424, s244948 (`runs/traces/vh-{nocover24,navtilt24}/`)
Notes: The entry above claims every downgrade was the share arithmetic drowning the mix in artillery. The tables say otherwise: the down seeds' loss compositions are broadly baseline-shaped (s16180 artillery 25 vs 34 of ~200 losses; s8128 simply a longer bloodier match), while the RESCUES are the artillery story (s42424 massed 48 and turned wiped into won; s244948 won so fast it lost a third of baseline's units). Corrected reading: the tilt causally converts naval LOSSES -- all six upgrades -- and on seeds that were already winning with fleets in view it changes early production enough to re-roll the coin, which at a 12/24 rung re-rolls roughly half. No magnitude error is in evidence; the cap arm is withdrawn before being built. Standing: net -2 does not clear the +4 adoption bar, the champion remains flame-nocover, and the tilt code stays in the tree -- inert without a fleet, structurally valuable on the loss modes, adoptable the day the bar is cleared on a bigger panel. The methodological finding is the durable one: a CONDITIONAL arm's effect concentrates in a seed subset, so 24-seed panels measure it against re-roll noise from the untargeted rest -- the instrument for conditional arms is larger panels, which is an infrastructure question (cheaper matches, the match service) before it is a strategy one.

## [2026-08-07] build | the match service, phase zero: the engine slot becomes a row you lock
Artifacts: `src/rw_bot/service/` (queue, worker, hooks; all gates green, 100% incl. branches), `scripts/submit_batch.py`, `scripts/match_worker.py`, `tests/test_service_*`, `runs/sweeps/service-smoke/` (the live proof), render_json lifted to `rw_bot.wire.ndjson` beside its parse-side sibling
Notes: The methodological finding of the arms night ("conditional arms need bigger panels, which is infrastructure before strategy") cashed in: the [[harness-match-service]] design's phase zero is built and live-verified against platform-postgres. One transaction shape carries it -- claim the oldest queued job FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, lease a free clone index inside the same transaction -- so two workers can never share a job or a clone dir, which retires the collision class that tonight's chain tangle demonstrated twice by hand. Jobs ride the harness's own codecs in and out (a queued job validates exactly like a sweep-file job), workers play through prepare_tree/prepare_clone/play_job unchanged so every artifact files where every reader expects, resubmission is resume (batch-label-seed key), and the reaper returns a dead worker's job and clone to the pool. Live smoke: submit 2, drain 2, verdicts filed, rows done, leases empty. Also paid tonight: the runner's clone index is ZERO-based (0 names .game-w1) and the pool argument says so; the guard's object-in-annotation and json-import bans pushed the service onto precise unions and the house emitter, which is how render_json found its way home to ndjson. Phase one (HTTP control plane, DB-mirrored blobs, freeze-verify-go) is scoped on the page.

## [2026-08-07] build+verdict-pending | phase one lands while phase zero carries its first production panel
Artifacts: `src/rw_bot/service/{http,submit}.py`, `scripts/match_service.py`, `queue.batch_status`, `tests/test_service_http.py` (+queue/cli extensions), all gates green; the live read `{"batch":"navpair48","queued":85,"running":5,"done":6,"failed":0}` over http://127.0.0.1:27501 while eight leased workers drain the panel
Notes: The control plane took the fleet page's shape rather than the design sketch's FastAPI -- the repo had already proven stdlib-http-with-a-pure-router, and the guard keeps it typed end to end (the same object-in-annotation and json-import bans that shaped the queue). One submission door for shells and wires alike: batch_config builds the stored configuration in one place, POST /batches is resubmission-safe by the same batch-label-seed key, and GET /batches/<name> is the counts-by-state read that used to require psql. The panel it reported on is the point of everything: navpair48, 48 fresh seeds under control and navtilt labels on ONE frozen tree (the tilt became a doctrine knob for exactly this), the conditional-arm measurement the 24-seed instrument could not deliver. Verdict when the queue drains.

## [2026-08-07] correction+fix | the first production panel finds the claim race: FOR UPDATE cannot lock a row that does not exist
Artifacts: `runs/navpair-workers.out` (the UniqueViolation traceback), `src/rw_bot/service/queue.py` claim rewritten, `tests/test_service_queue.py` thief-seam race tests, all gates green; live: leases 0-7 full, `{"queued":72,"running":8,"done":16,"failed":0}`
Notes: navpair48's first minutes killed three of eight workers on `clone_leases_pkey`, and the phase-zero log entry above contains the error in its own words: "lease a free clone index inside the same transaction" was implemented as read-with-FOR-UPDATE then insert -- but a FREE clone has no lease row, and a row lock cannot lock an absent row, so under READ COMMITTED every worker polling the empty table picked the same lowest index and every loser died on the primary key. Contained by construction (the transaction rolled back, no job orphaned, the panel just sagged to five lanes) and the diagnosis was pure phase-zero dividend: the lease table named the five living workers and pg_stat_activity cleared the DB of suspicion in two queries. The fix makes the insert the arbiter -- ON CONFLICT (clone_index) DO NOTHING RETURNING, walk the pool, a lost race is a decline like an empty queue -- with the race reproduced in the fake store via a thief seam (a rival lease landing between read and insert). Verified live MID-PANEL: three fixed-code workers joined five old-code workers on one lease table, filled clones 5-7 without incident, eight lanes restored. The wedged duplicate cohort (a prior launch attempt blocked on a dead stdout pipe, zero DB connections, zero leases) was culled by PID; detached workers now write through -u to per-worker files so the next incident has logs.

## [2026-08-07] build | results mirror, cards first: a batch's verdicts become a wire read
Artifacts: `queue.finish` stores the card + `batch_results`/`_verdict_of`, `worker` reads it via the new `read_card` hook, `GET /batches/<name>/results` streams NDJSON (one line per match: label, seed, state, verdict); all gates green; live: 96 rows served for navpair48 mid-panel
Notes: The design page's "results land in the same database" starts paying: finish mirrors the filed scorecard's text onto the job row (idempotent ALTER in bootstrap), and the paired-panel read -- the exact computation the navtilt verdict needs -- becomes one HTTP request instead of an artifact-tree walk. The live probe found a real gap on its first try: the door never bootstrapped, so its results route met a table an older writer shaped and died on the missing column; the door migrates at startup now, like every worker. Honesty preserved at both edges: a failed match mirrors an empty card (it filed only a .partial), and rows finished before the mirror existed read back with empty verdicts rather than reconstructed ones -- navpair48's early rows say so. The runs/ tree stays canonical; traces-as-compressed-blobs is the next mirror step.

## [2026-08-07] analysis | the scout war is background noise: scout losses do not discriminate outcomes, battleships and artillery do
Artifacts: aggregated killer tables from `runs/sweeps/vh-nocover24/` split by verdict (1244 losses in defeats, 1137 in wins) and `runs/sweeps/imp-nocover12/` (940)
Notes: The heloplus entry queued "the scout war" as the next behavioral lever because scouts top every killer table (x151 at Impossible, top at VH). The split-by-outcome table retires that reading before an arm is spent on it: scouts take 13.7% of unit losses in VH defeats and 11.4% in VH wins -- the same share either way, and the worker-loss share matches (11-17% at both rungs). Scouts are the most numerous enemy unit, so they top raw counts, but they are outcome-INDEPENDENT attrition: a scout-counter arm would optimize noise. What actually discriminates VH defeats from wins: battleShip 12.5% of losses in defeats vs 3.5% in wins (the naval wall, already targeted by navtilt -- navpair48 is ruling on it as this is written) and c_artillery 10.5% vs 3.9% (the enemy's 290-range gun, the mirror of our own naval answer). The win-side table is its own signature: c_turret_t2_gun 12.8% and commandCenter 9.9% top it, which is what losses look like when the army is assaulting a base. Standing docket after this: the behavioral lever at Impossible remains the s600-840 wave defense / fortress-creep choke-walk (trades cannot outrun 3.7x compounding), and at VH everything now waits on the navpair48 ruling.

## [2026-08-08] correction+fix | the port is a lease too: two random draws collided and killed two matches at the bind
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/imp-creep12/logs/imp-creep-s12345.log` (`BindException: Address already in use` at the agent's channel bind), `rw_bot.harness.clone.leased_port` + `make_argv(port=)` + `play_job` wiring, `queue.retry_failed` + `POST /batches/<name>/retries`; all gates green
Notes: The design page's phase-one sketch said workers hold "a lease on a clone index + port range" and phase zero leased only the clone -- the recipe kept its `random.randint(27600, 27999)` draw per match. Eight matches launching in one instant is a birthday problem in a 400-wide band (~7% per wave), and imp-creep12's first wave paid it twice: two agents drew the same port, the second bind failed, both matches filed .partial. The fix is the sentence the page already wrote: a clone ordinal IS an exclusive lease, so `leased_port` derives the channel port from it (base 27510 + ordinal -- above the fleet's 27500 and the door's 27501, below the recipe's random band, so leased and drawn ports cannot collide either) and `play_job` passes it whenever the game dir is a numbered clone; the single-match entry points keep the draw, silence-rule style. The second half is the verb the failure exposed: submission's resume key skips existing rows, so a failed row could never play again -- `retry_failed` requeues a batch's failures with clean outcome columns, served as `POST /batches/<name>/retries`. The two casualties requeue through it when the batch drains and replay on leased ports.

## [2026-08-08] verdict | navtilt rejected at conditional-arm scale: net -2 and five wins traded away, on 48 fresh seeds
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/navpair48/` (96 cards, 48 paired seeds, 0 failed; control 21W vs navtilt 16W), the paired table split by control battleship kills; the panel played entirely through the match service
Notes: The measurement the 24-seed instrument could not deliver, delivered: 8 up / 10 down / 30 flat, net -2 against the +4 adoption bar, and the number under the ranks is worse -- wins 21 -> 16. The navy split (39 of 48 seeds saw surface-ship kills on the control) holds the whole story: every mechanism from the vh-navtilt24 correction reproduced at double scale. The tilt causally converts naval LOSSES -- s100129 wiped->won, s100469 defeated->won, four more losses softened -- but on seeds the champion was already winning WITH ships in view it perturbs early production and re-rolls the coin, and at a >50% baseline win rate re-rolls are negative EV: five full wins collapsed (three all the way to wiped), which rank-movement flattens to -2 and the win column states honestly as -5. Land seeds: net 0, 7 of 9 flat -- the gate is clean; the tilt is inert without a fleet in sight. RULING: the champion stays flame-nocover, navtilt 0. The tilt code stays in the tree: provably inert when off, and its mechanism (range dominance over a seen fleet) is Impossible-relevant where battleships are the #2 killer and there are no wins to re-roll away. The wider law after ten measured modifications at two scales: the champion's razor-tuned mix is a local optimum that punishes every perturbation, permanent or conditional -- the remaining VH gap (21/48 on fresh seeds) is not reachable by composition surgery, and the docket moves to the rungs and maps beyond it. Also closed: the ghost match. worker-1 died overnight mid-play holding navtilt-s100313; the fresh fleet's first poll reaped and replayed it unattended, and the pair it completed was flat -- the service's first full recovery, invisible in the verdict, which is the point.

## [2026-08-08] verdict | the choke-walk dies fastest of all: 0/12, and the community turtle claim closes at Impossible
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/imp-creep12/` (12 cards, 0W/6 defeated/6 wiped; the two port-collision casualties replayed clean through the retry verb on leased ports 27511/27512); three-way survival vs `imp-nocover12` and `imp-fortress12`
Notes: The community claim's last unmeasured half, measured: fortress + creep 50 (the hold-point walk with its every-third-structure repair bays, the choke-walk cycle the guides actually describe) survives a MEAN 2776 samples -- below the static fortress's 2813 and far below the plain-fighting champion's 3380, worse than the champion on 10 of 12 seeds (the two exceptions are +19 and +30, noise). The pre-certification bastion mechanism reproduced exactly on the certified harness: every credit in the wall is a credit out of the army during the s600-840 wave window, and at 3.7x compounding the opponent never faces that allocation choice. RULING: the community turtle system is refuted at Impossible in both halves, post-certification -- static roof (imp-fortress12) and walking choke (imp-creep12) both die faster than just fighting. What survives of the community corpus at this rung is nothing structural; the one lever class measured positive ANYWHERE that remains untested post-certification is the income mechanism itself (the retimed extractorT3 withhold took 4/6 first-ever wins on never-won VH seeds pre-certification, log 2026-07-31) -- compounding answered with compounding rather than masonry. That is the designed next arm. Service note: this 14-match day (12 + 2 retries) exercised submit, drain, crash-reap-replay, retry and the leased ports end to end -- the instrument is no longer the bottleneck; the strategy is.

## [2026-08-08] verdict | the ladder funds its T3s and loses anyway: the income mechanism closes at Impossible, 0/12
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/imp-ladder12/` (12 cards: 0W/4 defeated/8 wiped, 0 failed; mean survival 3078 vs the champion's 3380, better 5 / worse 7), per-card `upgrade:extractorT3 asked/got` ledger
Notes: The last lever class measured positive anywhere, measured at the rung it was aimed at -- and the mechanical half WORKED: extractorT3 funded 1-2 times in EVERY match (the VH counter-measurement's whole complaint was "asked thousands of times, funded never"), including s173205's 4459-sample run, the longest Impossible survival on the ledger. It still went 0/12, because the arithmetic differs from VH in the one way that matters: at VH the withhold broke a worth CEILING on seeds whose matches were long stalemates; at Impossible the match is a race against a s600-840 wave train, the withhold's army pauses spend survival to buy income, and one or two T3 conversions compound nowhere near fast enough against an opponent compounding at 3.7x from a full economy. RULING: income_ladder stays off the champion at this rung. The post-certification Impossible ledger now reads 0/47 across all three lever classes with mechanisms on record -- trades (imp-nocover12), masonry static and walking (imp-fortress12, imp-creep12), and income (imp-ladder12). Every fixed-doctrine knob in the current vocabulary is measured; what remains is structural: attack THEIR economy in the opening (the raid verbs, unmeasured post-certification at this rung), change the terrain (the rung is only measured on duel_lake), or change the trade ratios themselves (micro: kiting, focus fire). The rung is not closed -- the knob drawer is.

## [2026-08-08] fix+launch | the door learns a connection is not forever, and the behavioral screen goes to the fleet
Artifacts: `scripts/match_service.py` (connection-per-request via an opener the server carries; startup bootstrap on its own connection), all gates green; `doctrines/flame-{kite,flee30,medics2,strike5,riposte,raid8}.doctrine` + `sweeps/imp-hands84.txt` (7 labels x 12 seeds, one frozen tree, in-batch control)
Notes: Production found the service's third real gap: the door held ONE Postgres connection for its whole life, the server dropped it during an idle gap, and every later request died on the corpse (`OperationalError 10053`) -- including the submit of this very screen, which is how it was noticed. At this door's request rate a connection per request costs milliseconds and an idle door now holds nothing losable. The screen it delayed: imp-hands84, the post-certification behavioral sweep the knob-drawer closure pointed at. Six hands, each ONE knob from the champion, each with a recorded pre-certification hypothesis aimed exactly at unfavorable fights: speed-gated kite, flee at hp_floor 30, two medics, momentum strike 5000 (its earlier "refutation" was retracted for broken determinism), riposte, and raid 8 (the heavier-party question the v2 raid measure left open). Plus the in-batch control, because cross-batch comparison stays dead. The asymmetry that makes this screen cheap where every VH arm was expensive: at a 0-win rung a perturbation cannot re-roll away a win -- every hand is pure question, measured in survival and any first win. 84 matches, one submission, paired per-seed.

## [2026-08-08] fix | connection lifecycle, the whole audit: between claim and finish a worker holds a lease row, not a socket
Artifacts: `src/rw_bot/service/worker.py` (one connection per queue interaction: bootstrap, each poll, each finish -- none held across a match), `tests/test_service_worker.py::test_no_connection_is_held_while_a_match_plays`; all gates green
Notes: The door's dropped-connection fix prompted the honest question -- is the lifecycle right EVERYWHERE -- and the audit found the same disease in the component where it costs more: run_worker opened one connection at startup and held it across its whole life, including the 20-40 minutes a match blocks. A server-side drop during a match would kill finish() on the corpse and orphan the job until the reaper (a plausible read of worker-1's overnight death, though its missing card says the match itself also died). The rule now stated and tested: no connection spans a long block. The worker opens one for bootstrap, one per reap-claim poll, one per finish, each closed before the next block; the crash window between claim and finish is covered by what already covers it, the lease and the reaper. The test asserts the property itself -- the connection is closed at the moment play_job runs -- not just the call counts. The running imp-hands84 fleet carries the old code (loaded at launch, harmless); every later fleet gets the lifecycle.

## [2026-08-08] build | the first trajectory-gated response: navtilt learns to read the scoreboard before the fleet
Artifacts: `doctrine.navtilt` bool -> three-mode int (`NAVTILT_OFF/ALWAYS/BEHIND`, RW-DOCTRINE-025 on the range), `counter.counter_composition(naval, behind)`, campaign reads `read_situation` at the tilt's call site (fogged scoreboard reads not-behind, erring toward leaving the mix alone); all gates green
Notes: Austin named the campaign's drift outright -- "we're looking for a single pattern that beats any and all enemies, rather than a bot that actually responds and adapts" -- and the ledger agrees with him: ten fixed-pattern arms rejected, and the ONE arm with causal rescues on its record was the conditional one, convicted only of firing while winning. This build is the correction's first piece: a response gated on trajectory as well as threat. Under NAVTILT_BEHIND the naval clause runs only while our army value sits under the strongest rival's (the engine's own unfogged broadcast, the same figures momentum already reads), which by navpair48's own table keeps the rescues -- every one fired from behind -- and forgoes the re-rolls -- every one fired while ahead. The design principle now stated for every future adaptive verb: adapt when losing, never touch what is winning. Measurement queued behind the hands84 screen: navgate96 (48 fresh seeds x control/navtilt-2 on one tree, +4 bar unchanged) and the deferred scout arm imp-scout24 (12 base + 12 scout-on at Impossible, in-batch control), both through the service.

## [2026-08-08] verdict | the hands screen: raid and kite are the first positive arms ever measured at Impossible, and four hands close
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/imp-hands84/` (7 labels x 12 seeds, paired vs in-batch control; 3 port-era failures replayed through the retry verb; one kite pair still landing as this is written, unable to move the ordering)
Notes: The post-certification behavioral screen, ruled: **raid8 +217 mean paired survival (6 up / 2 down)** and **kite +116 (5 up / 4 down)** are the first arms in forty-seven Impossible measurements to run POSITIVE against their control -- and they are exactly the two structural levers the knob-drawer closure predicted: touch their economy, and change the trade ratios. No hand won a match (the floor holds), but the screen sorted six hypotheses into two live and four closed: riposte 0 up / 10 down, -562 (stockpiling through waves at this rung is dying with a saved reserve), medics2 -418 (healers are credits not fighting), flee30 -110 and strike5 -104 (flat; the momentum window barely opens when their army never dips). The pre-certification hypothesis that designed the screen -- "reflexes lose at winning rungs; their case lives where fights are unfavorable" -- is now measured HALF-right: the kite hand pays where fights are unfavorable, the flee/heal hands do not, and the distinction is that kite keeps units FIRING while the others subtract them from the fight. Follow-up already queued through the service (imp-combo36): the two positive hands composed (raid 8 + kite 1) and the raid channel probed deeper (raid 12), each against a fresh in-batch control. Also of note: the whole screen survived a mid-panel Docker outage -- the door self-healed on its per-request connections, the parametric reaper requeued the orphans, and not one row was lost.

## [2026-08-08] verdict | the behind-gate halves the damage but keeps the disease: net -2 again, and the signal is named
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/navgate96/` (96 cards, 48 pairs, 0 failed): 3 up / 5 down / 40 flat, net -2, wins 21->19; ungated navpair48 beside it: 8 up / 10 down / 30 flat, net -2, wins 21->16
Notes: The gate WORKED as a volume knob -- movement fell from 18 pairs to 8 and the win damage halved (-5 to -2) -- and failed as a selector: when it fired, the same fraction of firings broke wins (four collapsed). The mechanism is now precise: at a 1.8x income rung `our_army < rival_army` is true for long stretches of matches the champion WINS, so an instantaneous army deficit is not "losing" -- it is the normal shape of a winning game against a subsidized opponent. The navpair rescues all came from seeds that literally DIED to fleets, and the sharp in-match analog of that condition is not a deficit but BLOOD: units of ours actually killed by WATER-movers. The Scorekeeper already tallies deaths by killer type and the intel layer already remembers which type names move on water, so the bloodied gate is a join of two existing records. Iteration three of the adaptive clause builds now: mode 2 becomes NAVTILT_BLOODIED -- the tilt arms only after the fleet has drawn blood (>=2 kills), which by construction cannot fire in a game the fleet never touched. The design language for adaptive verbs sharpens accordingly: gate on the failure mode itself, not on a proxy that correlates with it.

## [2026-08-08] verdict | the scout costs a thousand samples at Impossible: eyes close at the rung, and the reason names itself
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/imp-scout24/` (24 cards, 12 pairs, 0 failed): 1 up / 10 down / 1 flat, mean -1008 samples vs in-batch control, 0 wins
Notes: The deferred scout arm, ruled -- and it is the worst paired arm ever measured at the rung, worse than riposte's -562. The VH refutation (scouting lost on economy grounds, 2026-07-29) compounds at Impossible through the tilt: with scout on, the counter reads the REMEMBERED picture instead of gun-range contact, and against a 3.7x opponent the remembered picture is an enormous roster that keeps the unconditional air tilt firing all match -- the mix perturbs constantly, at the rung where the champion's opening is the only thing keeping it alive to s3300. Two seeds prove the mechanism's other half is real (s244948 +442 -- intel CAN pay), but the aggregate is unambiguous. The eyes are not wrong as machinery; feeding a fixed-rule tilt an ever-larger threat memory is wrong. The lesson mirrors navtilt's exactly: adaptive INPUT without a gated RESPONSE is perturbation, not adaptation. Scout stays 0 at Impossible; the intel layer's future is behind gated verbs, the pattern the bloodied tilt now carries.

## [2026-08-08] verdict | the raid channel peaks at 8 and refuses company: both combo arms negative, and the one-knob law goes behavioral
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/imp-combo36/` (36 cards, 0 failed): raidkite 2 up / 7 down / 3 flat, mean -204; raid12 2 up / 7 down / 3 flat, mean -214; vs raid8's +217 and kite's +98 alone (imp-hands84)
Notes: Both follow-ups to the screen's two positive hands, refuted in one batch. The composite (raid 8 + kite 1) is NEGATIVE though both components are positive alone -- the kite reflex and the raid party interfere, plausibly because a raiding unit that steps back out of its chaser's reach is a raider that never commits to the extractor it was sent to kill. And raid 12 is negative where raid 8 paid +217, so the channel is not monotone: eight units hurt their economy without gutting our line, twelve gut our line. The composition law that closed the VH knob drawer -- every entry reweights a razor-tuned whole -- now has a behavioral twin: HANDS do not compose freely either, and each combination is its own measurement, not an inference. Standing: raid 8 alone is the only adoptable behavioral gain at Impossible, on 12 seeds once. Before it becomes the rung's reference configuration it replicates at double scale: imp-raid8x48 queued behind navblood96 (24 seeds x base/raid8, the 12 canonical plus 12 fresh primes, in-batch control as always).

## [2026-08-08] verdict | the blood gate churns wins one-for-one, and the tilt thread closes with its laws banked
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/navblood96/` (96 cards, 48 pairs, 0 failed): 11 up / 13 down / 24 flat, net -2, wins 20->19; SIX wiped->won conversions against ELEVEN won-seed collapses
Notes: The third calibration is the decisive one because its failure is symmetric: the blood-gated tilt CREATED ten wins -- six full wiped->won flips, rescues no prior calibration approached -- and DESTROYED eleven, for net -1 wins and net -2 rank. The navy subgroup alone is exactly break-even (11/11/21, wins +1). The three-panel arc now proves something no single panel could: ungated (-2), deficit-gated (-2), blood-gated (-2) -- every scalar gate lands at the same net, because the trigger populations overlap. Naval contact occurs in nearly every match; naval doom in few; and a present-tense signal cannot tell a game the fleet WILL doom from a game the fleet merely touched. The response itself is the problem: mass artillery is match-reshaping, so arming it requires predicting the future, not reading the present. RULING: the tilt thread closes. Champion stays flame-nocover navtilt 0 at every rung; the three-mode machinery stays in the tree, inert and tested, awaiting a driver that can actually predict fleet doom (the ML layer's certified-card corpus is the obvious candidate, someday). Banked from the thread: laws 3, 4, and the sharpest one -- a response's blast radius must match its trigger's precision. Small reversible responses can gate on present signals; match-reshaping responses cannot.

## [2026-08-09] measure | the ML pipeline's first real question: fleet doom is invisible in what the trace records
Artifacts: `runs/ml/fleetdoom/` (dataset builder, covenant_ml runner, the 144-match CSV): logreg test AUC 0.548, lightgbm 0.547, fold spread 0.29-0.71 -- chance
Notes: The pipeline's first use on a bot question, and the question was the one the tilt thread died asking: can ANYTHING in the first 800 samples predict "the fleet will doom this game"? Dataset: the 144 certified VH control matches (48 seeds x 3 independent trees), label = lost with surface-ship kills (base rate 0.43), features = 72 shape statistics of every trace column's early window, split GROUPED BY SEED because spawn geometry repeats across trees. Both backends land at AUC ~0.55 with fold spread that brackets 0.5 -- no detectable signal at this corpus size. The finding is about the DATA, not the idea: the trace records our economy's shape (army, credits, income, rival worth) and nothing about WHAT the enemy is -- no composition, no layers, no naval contact timing. Fleet doom, if predictable at all, lives in enemy-composition dynamics the bot OBSERVES every tick and never writes down. The actionable half: the trace gains enemy-shape columns (naval/air counts in sight, kills on us by layer) as a small recorded-forever change, and every future batch then feeds the dataset for free. Until then, the predictive driver the tilt machinery waits for has nothing to learn from -- which also retroactively justifies closing the thread rather than iterating gates: even the pipeline cannot see doom in the present tense of what we record.

## [2026-08-09] build | the trace learns to see the enemy: navy_seen, air_seen, navy_blood appended
Artifacts: `src/rw_bot/policy/{trace,recorder,counter,campaign}.py` (Tick +3 columns, `layer_counts`, unconditional fleet memory), all gates green; columns APPENDED after `workers` so autopsy's 4-13 and the exporter's first fifteen survive by position
Notes: The fleet-doom ML pass's actionable half, landed: every trace now carries the enemy's shape per sample -- WATER-movers in sight, fliers in sight, and the death ledger's cumulative naval kills on us -- read from live contact regardless of the counter knob. The moment a fleet first draws blood becomes a column read instead of an inference, and every batch the service plays from here feeds the fleet-doom dataset for free. The re-ask has its trigger written down: once a few hundred column-bearing matches accumulate, the classifier runs again on features that can actually carry the answer.

## [2026-08-09] build | decisions become a column: the events stream lands in the trace
Artifacts: `trace.Tick.events` (+ recorder/campaign wiring), `dispatching.fight` (the loop's fighting tail moved to the sending arm it always belonged to, closing a C901 and a 600-line cap in one motion; the four senders it wrapped went private); all gates green
Notes: The second recording gap from the audit, closed: every trace row now carries the decision codes issued since the previous row -- T the tilt changed the mix, R a raid drafted, M a forced march moved, S the strike window stood open, C the closer held. Every tilt postmortem to date reconstructed firing from composition side-effects; the three-calibration arc would have been three direct reads. Cut at recording boundaries so the loop's order never bends for the record, and proven end to end by a test that opens the momentum window through a real scripted fall and reads the S off the row. The architecture came out cleaner than it went in: fight() lives in dispatching because only the campaign/dispatching pair may send, and the guard that names that pair is what chose the split.

## [2026-08-09] verdict | raid8 retracted: the same twelve seeds flip from +217 to -198 across trees, and law nine is the price of almost adopting a mirage
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/imp-raid8x48/` (47 cards + 1 retry in flight, unable to move the mean): 23 pairs, 4 up / 8 down / 11 flat, mean -158; canonical-12 split -198 vs hands84's +217 ON THE SAME SEEDS; fresh-11 split -114
Notes: The replication panel did exactly what it existed to do -- it caught a mirage one step before adoption. raid 8's +217 was measured once, on one frozen tree; on a fresh tree the identical seeds read -198, a full sign reversal that seed identity cannot explain. The mechanism: a paired panel controls within-tree noise (both arms share one tree), but the ARM-vs-CONTROL difference itself rides the tree's chaos -- an early divergence cascades differently per tree, and a modest real effect (if any) drowns in tree-to-tree variance. LAW NINE: an effect that does not replicate across trees is a property of the tree, not the doctrine; adoption requires a fresh-tree replication, which is now the standing bar. RULING: raid8 is retracted, the Impossible reference configuration stays flame-nocover raid 3, and the rung's certified ledger returns to zero adoptable gains -- honestly. The wider implication reaches back through the whole behavioral screen: kite's +98 carries the same one-tree caveat and is hereby downgraded to unreplicated. What survives unconditionally is the negative results (they replicated across arms and trees all week) and the instrument: this panel cost one submission and ran unattended, which is why the discipline that caught the mirage was affordable at all.

## [2026-08-09] build | the queue learns urgency: priority claims, and a strategic question stops waiting behind a data batch
Artifacts: `queue.reprioritize` + `priority` column (claim ORDER BY priority, id; lower sooner; idempotent migration), `POST /batches/<name>/priority`; all gates green
Notes: The terrain screen sat behind vhdoom96's 66 background data matches because the queue was strictly first-come -- the right default and the wrong law. Priority is a per-job int defaulting to 100, bumpable per batch on unclaimed rows only (a running match is already someone's), claimed lowest-first with id breaking ties. The door verb makes it one POST from any session. First use: the four terrain batches and the cadence probe jump the data batch, which keeps filling in the gaps behind them.

## [2026-08-09] verdict | terrain closes as a parameter: duel_lake was already the champion's BEST map
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/imp-{duel12b,bridge12,straight12,hills12}/` (48 cards, 2 retries clean, priority-claimed ahead of the data batch): duel 3325 / bridge 2811 / straits 2317 / hills 2281 mean survival, 0/48 wins
Notes: The last zero-code lever, measured, and the hope inverted: every alternative map is WORSE than open duel_lake, each for a mechanism the week already named. Fire Bridge (-514): a choke pays the defender who STANDS at it, and the bot has no hold-the-choke verb -- its army gathers at the anchor while their army crosses the funnel freely (the pre-cert bridge autopsy, replicated at scale). Dire Straits (-1008): more water is more of the navy the composition cannot fight -- the naval wall, amplified by geography. Hills (-1044): the map ships with FOG DISABLED, so the counter tilt reads the opponent's entire roster all match -- the imp-scout24 lesson (ungated input is perturbation) reproduced by a map property instead of a knob. Also duel12b re-anchored the floor on a third independent tree: 3325 vs 3380 vs 3293 -- the duel_lake baseline is a stable fact, which is exactly what made these deltas readable. RULING: terrain-as-parameter is closed at this rung; duel_lake is retained as the reference map BECAUSE it is the most favorable, and the "any map" goal now reads harder than the duel_lake goal, not easier. The win path narrows to what it always was underneath: capabilities (the cadence probe claims next) and prediction (the data batch resumes behind it). A choke-HOLDING verb -- stand the army at the funnel the way creep held turrets at it -- is the one build this screen argues FOR, since bridge's death shape (11 defeated, structures standing) says the base outlives the army there.

## [2026-08-09] verdict | triple cadence moves nothing: reaction rate was never the binding constraint
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/imp-fast12/` (12 cards, 0 failed, lockstep 25, samples budget 3x): 0/12, paired frame delta -17,440 vs the duel anchor (4 up / 8 down), i.e. mildly negative-to-noise
Notes: The whole bot at triple reaction rate -- every verb deciding every 25 frames instead of 75 -- and the game-time survival is if anything SHORTER. Cross-tree caveat applies (law nine), but the reading law nine licenses is exactly the one that matters: there is no large effect here, and only large effects are real at this rung. The pre-cert lurk finding ("the faster clock softened the treadmill without flipping it") generalizes to the full bot: the decisions themselves, not their frequency, are the ceiling -- and a plausible mechanism for the negative tail is the perturbation family's, since 3x decisions is 3x re-decisions. Cadence closes as a capability lever. The docket after tonight's four rulings (terrain x3 + cadence): one evidence-backed build remains from the screen -- the choke-HOLDING verb bridge argued for -- and the prediction thread waits on the sighted corpus (vhdoom96 resumed, 49/96).

## [2026-08-09] build | the choke-holding verb: the reserve learns to stand where the map narrows
Artifacts: doctrine int `hold` (0-100, RW-DOCTRINE-026) -> `rally_post` computes the point at hold percent of the anchor-to-mirror line (creep's own geometry, pure and fogless), threaded WaveController -> campaign -> play; rally_post returns points now, not structures; all 100 presets carry `hold 0`; all gates green
Notes: The terrain screen's one constructive argument, built: Fire Bridge read 514 samples worse than open ground with the army at home while their waves crossed the funnel freely, and its death shape said the base was never where the fight was lost. `hold N` posts the reserve at N percent of the line to the mirrored start -- at 50 on a symmetric bridge map, the funnel's mouth -- taking precedence over `forward` because a doctrine says where the army stands with one field. Measured next as imp-bridgehold24 (base|hold50 ON THE BRIDGE, in-batch control, priority-claimed); the fresh-tree replication bar applies to any positive before adoption.

## [2026-08-09] verdict | hold v1 refuted with its mechanism photographed: the post trickles, and the trickle dies
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/imp-bridgehold24/` (24 cards, 0 failed): 3 up / 6 down / 3 flat, mean -264, 0 wins; spatial check on s12345's loss tables -- 12 early deaths vs base's 7, early-death centroid moved from (1303,550) to (1272,798), mid-map on the walk to the post
Notes: The choke-holding verb, measured on the map that argued for it, and refuted in its first form -- with the death positions the enemy-shape recording era makes readable saying exactly why: units rally to the funnel's mouth ONE BY ONE as production trickles them out, and the crossing waves eat each arrival before the next lands. The reserve's unexamined virtue at the base was never the base -- it was that units MASS where they are born. This is the anti-trickle law wearing a new coat (the all-in chain, creep v1, the income posts all died of it), now confirmed for defensive posture too: a forward position is only as good as the mass that occupies it, and a gather point is not a mass. Candidate v2 designs are logged, not built -- mass-at-base-then-walk-as-a-group, or the post taken by a released wave rather than the reserve -- because iterating postures against tree noise on a map already 500 samples worse than the reference is not where the next large effect lives. The verb stays in the tree behind hold 0, its geometry tested, awaiting a design that moves masses instead of individuals. What the panel proved positively: the new columns work -- this is the first ruling in the campaign whose mechanism was READ off death positions instead of inferred.

## [2026-08-09] measure | FLEET DOOM IS PREDICTABLE: AUC 0.80, precision 0.82, at a deployable moment with lead time
Artifacts: `runs/ml/fleetdoom/` (96 sighted matches, 43/53 labels, 96 seed groups, sample-2000 window): lightgbm test AUC 0.800 (worst fold 0.707), precision 0.820 / recall 0.622; logreg 0.778; the 800-sample window read chance because only 7/96 matches had SEEN a ship by then -- at 2000, 69/96 have seen and 53/96 have bled
Notes: The campaign's first positive predictive result, and the first payout of the sighted-era columns: the fleet-doom question that three hand-written gates answered at net -2 is answerable by a model at precision 0.82 -- four of five activations on genuinely doomed games -- from features the bot computes in-match (early-window shape statistics + naval contact and blood timing), at a prediction point (~sample 2000) that precedes the earliest doom deaths by 600+ samples. The window lesson mattered as much as the features: the first re-ask failed because the fleet had not ARRIVED by sample 800, which the timing features themselves diagnosed. Every fold grouped by seed, every fold above 0.70. LAW NINE APPLIES: this is one corpus on one tree, and nothing gets built on it until vhdoom96b (96 fresh primes, fresh tree, submitted) replicates the AUC. If it holds: the deployment design is the tilt's mode 3 -- the model as the driver the three-calibration arc proved no scalar could be -- with logreg's coefficient form available dependency-free if lightgbm is too heavy for the loop.

## [2026-08-09] fix | zombie engines hold leased ports: the launcher clears its own port before binding
Artifacts: `scripts/make/play.ps1` (pre-launch port-clear, scoped to java holders only); 3 vhdoom96b failures requeued clean
Notes: The port-lease design's blind spot, found by the third Docker outage: a worker killed mid-match leaves its ENGINE alive, the job requeues, and the replay lands on the same clone -- whose leased port the zombie still holds. Three matches died at the bind before the pattern showed. The launcher now clears the port itself, scoped to java processes only (nothing else can legitimately hold a match port, so a java holder is always an orphan; any other holder is left for the bind to report loudly). play.ps1 reads from the working tree even under frozen-tree matches, so the guard applies to every future launch immediately. Also on the record: this outage happened with 48GB free -- Docker Desktop's instability is not RAM.

## [2026-08-09] verdict | the doom signal replicates at 0.75 cross-tree, and its precision tells the truth about deployment
Artifacts: `runs/ml/fleetdoom/fleetdoom_b.csv` (96 fresh-tree matches, 41/55 labels, 0 failed through Docker's fourth death); within-B grouped CV AUC 0.636 (wide folds); train-A-test-B AUC 0.751 tight across runs (0.731-0.766); cross-tree precision plateaus at 0.68 from threshold 0.6 to 0.8
Notes: Law nine's second application, and this one PASSES where raid8 sign-flipped: a model fitted on one tree scores a wholly unseen tree's matches at 0.75 consistently -- the fleet-doom signal is real and transfers. The regression from 0.80 is the honest cost of replication (winner's curse), and the operating curve is the finding that matters: cross-tree precision saturates at roughly two-thirds no matter how strict the threshold, so about one armed match in three would not have been doomed. Two-thirds precision is far beyond every scalar gate (the blood latch fired in MOST wins) and far short of certainty -- which puts the mode-3 question exactly where this campaign puts questions: on a panel. The deployment plan: export the corpus-A model in a dependency-free form, drive navtilt's clause from its score at the sample-2000 read, and measure blind on fresh seeds with the in-batch control, +4 bar, fresh-tree replication on any positive. Expectation calibrated by arithmetic: at 0.68 precision / 0.63 recall the panel is a genuine coin worth flipping properly, not a sure thing. Also on the record: the corpus that decided this survived four Docker deaths, one friendly-fire reap (the recipe now says short-reap only when zero engines live), and cost three retry commands total.

## [2026-08-09] build | mode 3: the first learned decision enters the bot
Artifacts: `src/rw_bot/policy/doom.py` (DoomModel decode, DoomWatch feature parity, DoomLatch one-decision scoring), `models/fleetdoom.ndjson` (90 standardized-logistic coefficients, fitted THROUGH the watch on corpus A -- cross-tree AUC 0.755 / precision 0.69 / recall 0.83 at thr 0.7 via the same class the loop scores with), `NAVTILT_PREDICTED` threaded doctrine -> counter -> campaign -> play; all gates green, the shipped model file proven decodable by the end-to-end test
Notes: The driver law eight demanded, deployed: the naval clause's mode 3 arms on a doom PREDICTION, latched once at the model's own window (~sample 2000) -- one decision for a match-reshaping response, never a flap. Train/serve parity is structural: the exporter fits on features computed by the very DoomWatch the loop feeds, so the arithmetic cannot drift between the dataset and the match, and the export run reproduced the pipeline's cross-tree numbers exactly as its parity proof. The loop pays nothing when the knob is off -- the model file is read only when a doctrine says navtilt 3. What this is NOT yet: an improvement. It is the first learned decision wired into the bot, and whether a 0.69-precision oracle converts naval doom without re-rolling wins is the navdoom96 panel's question, on fresh seeds, against the +4 bar, with fresh-tree replication owed on any positive.

## [2026-08-10] verdict | the learned driver fails too, and the four-calibration arc reaches its real conclusion: the response was the flaw
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/navdoom96/` (96 cards, 48 pairs, 1 guard-halt retried clean): 4 up / 9 down / 35 flat, net -5, wins 26->22; NAVY 42 pairs net -2 (2 wiped->won against steady collapses), LAND 6 pairs 0 up / 3 down -- the false-fire tax, exactly where 0.69 precision predicted it
Notes: The first learned decision in the bot's history, measured like everything else -- and rejected harder than the hand-written gates it was built to replace. The arc now reads: ungated -2, deficit-gated -2, blood-gated -2, PREDICTED -5. With a genuinely predictive driver (cross-tree AUC 0.75, the first law-nine survivor) the navy subset still nets -2: even CORRECTLY targeted arming converts and collapses in roughly equal measure, and every false fire on a healthy game is pure loss. The conclusion stops being about triggers: THE RESPONSE ITSELF is unprofitable. Mass artillery reshaping buys rescues worth about as much as the wins it risks, so no trigger quality -- not blood, not prediction, not perfection -- can push it net positive. LAW TEN: measure the response's conditional payoff BEFORE investing in its trigger; a response that breaks even when correctly armed is a dead end at any precision. RULING: navtilt 0 permanently; the tilt-response thread closes with prejudice. What survives, fully banked: the doom model (a replicated 0.75-AUC predictor of naval doom, available to drive anything), the mode-3 wiring (any future response can ride a model through it), the DoomWatch/latch machinery, and the discipline that measured its own most sophisticated candidate to death in one panel. The bot still plays exactly as it did -- and the system that keeps it honest has never been stronger.

## [2026-08-10] probe | the water opens: terrain-by-attempt sites a sea factory, and the whole naval roster is tier one
Artifacts: `scripts/sea_probe.py` + live run on duel_lake s424242: fraction 0.20 refused, 0.25 ACCEPTED at (1090,1590); the completed factory offers builderShip $500, gunBoat $300, lightSub $450, missileShip $900, hovercraft $600, battleShip $1500 and attackSubmarine $800 -- ALL available at tier one -- and the ordered submarine entered the world
Notes: The naval theater's gate, opened in one probe. The bot has no terrain map and never needed one: offer the engine a build order along the anchor-to-mirror line and its accept-or-ignore IS the terrain sensor -- dry land swallowed forty offers in silence, the first wet fraction grew a factory. The pricing that aimed this (log 2026-08-10 earlier): the enemy's fleet core declares "No anti-air or anti-sub" while the $800 attackSubmarine outranges it (250 vs 240) submerged and untouchable, and also outranges the missileShip's "light anti-sub torpedos" (200) -- a hard counter by the engine's own stat sheet, with positive expected value WHEN CORRECTLY ARMED, which is the law-ten test the artillery tilt failed. Now buildable: the siting rule the probe prototyped moves into the placement layer (a water-siting walk for "can only be built on water" types), naval goals ride the existing production machinery, and the first arm is champion + seaFactory + attackSubmarines, priced, probed, and panel-ready. The bot's first new THEATER since the campaign began.

Artifacts: `src/rw_bot/policy/navy.py` (Shipyard: the probe's terrain-by-attempt walk as a saving channel -- one candidate at a time, PATIENCE per candidate, claim-or-withhold on the factory's price), submarines via `Medic("attackSubmarine")` unchanged (a sub is a hire whose producer floats), doctrine int `navy` (RW-DOCTRINE-027) threaded campaign -> play, 102 presets carry `navy 0`; all gates green

The arm: flame-navy2 -- the champion plus a sea factory and two standing attack submarines, about 2,600 credits of water -- against the in-batch control on 48 fresh seeds, the +4 bar, replication owed on any positive. For the first time this week the response being measured was priced positive-when-armed before a line of its trigger existed.

## [2026-08-10] fix | the walk claimed itself to death: navy96 voided, one claim one order shipped
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/navy96/` VOIDED (the arm read 0/27 wins against the control's 13 with `spend navy:seaFactory asked 369 got 369 spent 369,000` -- the walk claimed the factory's price EVERY TICK and the economy never existed: extractors 0, workers 0, income 0/s); `navy.py` rewritten to one claim and one order per candidate, patience watching the roster, the NEWEST builder taking the trip; all gates green
Notes: The house channel pattern, learned the hard way for the second time this campaign: Medic claims once per outstanding order and my walk claimed per tick, which is not a navy, it is a 369,000-credit bonfire wearing one. The ledger caught it in a single line -- the spend table exists for exactly this. Also fixed in the same pass: the walk took builders[0], the OPENING's builder, and dragging it lakeward is dragging the opening with it; builders[-1] (the newest hire) takes the trip now. The panel's remaining matches drain as data about the broken build and are marked void; navy96b resubmits on the fixed code. The theater's pricing is untouched by any of this -- the submarine's stat-sheet case never depended on the walk being competent.

## [2026-08-10] fix | navy96b voided too: the expander stole the builder back, and v3 re-sends every tick
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/navy96b/` VOIDED for the theater question (economy healthy, net -2 with four wiped->won moves -- but `navy:seaFactory asked ~2000 got 5-9` and no factory ever stood: the single build order was overridden by the expander's re-task a tick later); `navy.py` v3 (claim once, re-send the order every tick -- the navy block sends AFTER the expander, so its order lands last and wins the builder, which is exactly why the probe's every-tick re-send worked); navy96c submitted on the v3 tree
Notes: Two voided panels bought the walk's two lessons: the budget is per-tick and a claim per tick is a bonfire (v1); the engine holds one order per unit and whoever sends LAST holds the unit (v2). v3 is the probe's own behavior, which was ground truth all along -- claim once for the books, re-send for the engine. Interesting residue in the voided panel: navy96b's arm was effectively "champion minus a withheld ~1000 and a wandering builder", and it read net -2 with real rescues both ways -- consistent with a modest handicap, not a collapse, which bounds the walk's overhead as small once it works.

Notes: The gunship kill named the missing piece and the stat sheet had already assigned the job: the missile ship is the only hull that shoots air, so a fleet without one is a free kill queue -- the water's own aa_cover argument. ## [2026-08-10] fix | eight planners are CPU tenants: heavy co-located jobs starve the sample stream and matches die by socket timeout
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/navy96d/navy2-s106391.partial` (and 7 siblings): `TimeoutError` in `channel.next_sample()` -- the planner's socket read outwaited the engine's 250ms sample stream; two failure bursts (20:36, 20:41-20:47) both inside the window where a wiki embedding index rebuild (fp32 on CPU) and a vault audit ran beside 8 fast-forwarding engines; one burst also wedged an engine at the menu (5 fails in one minute, `.game-w8`, autosave rename failure at the same timestamp); all 8 retried via the verb, 0 failed outstanding
Notes: RAM was never tight (20+ GB free) -- the resource that ran out was CPU headroom for the engine's sampling cadence. The fleet's real capacity constraint at 64GB is now scheduling, not memory: heavy CPU jobs (embedding models, audits, big test suites) are fleet-hostile while lanes are fast-forwarding and belong in the gaps between panels. Failures of this shape say nothing about the doctrine; the label-scoped priority kept the retried submarine matches at the front of the line.

## [2026-08-10] fix | navy96d voided by its own diff: the shipyard sent before the expander and no factory ever stood
Artifacts: `runs/sweeps/navy96d/` VOIDED for the theater question, fourth panel running -- the ledger's `navy:seaFactory asked 1 got 1` looked like success, but the engine logs show every one of 48 arm matches walking all eleven fractions with ~41 re-sends each and the builder re-tasked every tick: the v4 edit moved `shipyard.establish`'s send ABOVE the expander's, un-learning v3's own lesson (whoever sends last holds the builder). navy96c's v3 walk, by the same per-match classification, was accepted in 24 of 26. Fix is a one-line move plus the regression lock the ordering never had (`test_the_shipyards_order_lands_after_the_expanders`); navy96e submitted on the fixed tree, 48 fresh interleaved pairs at priority 10, navy96d's void remainder dropped to filler.
Notes: The instrument caught its own lie in one place and told the truth in another: the budget ledger records what was PAID, the engine log records what was OBEYED, and only their disagreement named the bug. Panel five is the first in which a submarine can actually exist. The interleaved pair ordering also ships here, so the paired read fills in seed by seed instead of arm by arm.

## [2026-08-10] fix | navy96e voided in its first hour: the walk's builder rotated with every hire and nobody ever reached the shore

## [2026-08-10] fix | law eleven and the mechanism census: every card now testifies to what EXISTED, and no panel runs before a pilot proves the mechanism
Artifacts: `owned peak` line in every future scorecard (Scorekeeper tracks peak simultaneous count per owned type per observation; MatchReport carries and renders it; the end-state tables cannot testify for a structure that stood and died, which is the exact blindness five navy panels ran on); law 11 in the ledger: mechanism before measurement -- a pilot match's card must prove the arm's mechanism executed before its panel is submitted, and probes ship whole; tests: the census remembers what stood and died, plus the three play line-lists extended
Notes: The protocol change the five voided panels bought. The pilot is cheap (one arm match, minutes) and reads against a line the match itself produces, so a phantom success can no longer survive a card read -- the ledger says paid, the census says existed, and a panel needs both.

The stat-sheet hard counter was real (six rescues prove it) and insufficient, because battleships were never our top killer problem -- tempo was. The theater closes honestly: capability banked (walk, pin, guard, headcount all regression-locked and reusable on any water map), economics refuted at Very Hard on duel_lake. Six panels, three stacked bugs, one true measurement. flame-nocover remains champion.

## [2026-08-11] fix | the dense margin ships: one bounded number per match, validated against the era it would have shortened
Artifacts: `scripts/margin.py` + `tests/test_margin.py` -- margin = verdict anchor (+2/+1/-1/-2) + pressure (worst dip over rival peak, the erosion law's own figure) + tempo (fast wins and long losses count), bands constructed so ranking by margin can never disagree with ranking by verdict; per-batch per-arm means and paired deltas beside the win deltas they sharpen; all gates green. Validation against history: navy96f reads -1.212 (sd 3.281, ~2.6 sem below zero) agreeing with its -8 rejection; the four tilt panels read -0.16/-0.37/-0.27/-0.34 -- all within noise of zero, which is the "breaks even, trades wins for survivals" conclusion the win-bit needed four panels and three calibration arcs to establish.
Notes: The margin is the prerequisite the automated-search idea was waiting for: an evaluation cheap enough per panel (same matches, ~20x the signal per match) that a bandit over doctrine combinations becomes affordable. It is an ANALYSIS layer only -- verdicts still rule adoption, and the +4 win bar stands; the margin's job is triage and sizing, not replacing the bar. The navy-era queue also drained completely today: every batch played, zero failures outstanding, fleet idle at four lanes.

Artifacts: `rw_bot/harness/search.py` (candidates from a knob space, seeded pair sampling per law two, paired-delta scoring, deterministic halving) + `scripts/search.py` (the driver: writes variant doctrines, submits interleaved rounds through the queue -- every arm against ONE shared control on fresh seeds -- polls, scores by margin, halves, reports a graduation order) + `rw_bot/harness/margin.py` (the margin's pure core, moved from the script so the driver imports a library, CLI unchanged); end-to-end test drives the whole schedule against the in-memory queue; all gates green
Notes: The arm ladder industrialized: v1 space is six knobs around the champion (flame, close, raid, tech, medics, decoys), 16 candidates (10 singles + 6 sampled pairs), schedule 8-then-16 pairs, ~280 matches for a full search -- the manual ladder spent that on three arms. The discipline is unchanged and explicit in both modules: margin is triage, graduation is a full win-bar panel plus fresh-tree replication, the search proposes and the bar disposes.

## [2026-08-11] fix | the driver outlasts its fourth Docker crash: patient connects, stall warnings, and a clean resume
Artifacts: Docker's fourth crash killed the first vhsearch1 driver mid-poll (`psycopg ConnectionTimeout`) and took workers creepw-1/4 with it; recovery was the standing recipe (surgical requeue by dead worker name, `make fleet-up`, priorities re-set) and the round resumed at 6/136 with nothing lost. Hardening: `patient_connect` in the driver retries database outages forever with a named line (psycopg identified by module, not import -- the service keeps its DB behind an untyped Protocol seam and the driver honors that; any non-psycopg error still propagates), and `wait_for_batch` names a round nobody claims after three polls (the silent empty-fleet stall from launch day). Tests: outage-outlasted, bug-propagates, stall-named-once, stall-counter-resets; all gates green.
Notes: The driver now survives everything its queue rows survive. Relaunched on the hardened code; resubmission is idempotent so the round continues rather than restarting.

## [2026-08-13] probe | the artillery battery stands, converts, and draws blood -- but no ship ever tested it
Artifacts: `scripts/turret_probe.py` + live run on duel_lake s424242, difficulty 3, nothing else playing (`runs/turret-probe.log`): line fractions 0.22-0.16 refused, turret STANDS at 0.14 (1040,1780); the standing turret offers all four forks -- gun $1000, **artillery $1600**, flame $700, lightning $2200 -- and the conversion completes in place (BATTERY STANDS, hp 1000); DREW BLOOD on a hoverTank (150 -> 78 hp) at range 194 with the engine's own `damaged_by` naming the battery; died to the first air wave (gunShip and three interceptors in reach, and `hits_air` is false for the fork) before any ship sailed; the probe's own player wiped ~sample 1275.
Notes: The naval hole's cheapest candidate response (the theater closed at -8, the tilt at -5, and "do nothing" stands by default): the artillery fork's 350 reach against the battleship's 240. Law eleven's mechanism half is now proven live -- builder places `c_turret_t1`, the four-way fork converts in place through the same produce-on-self the flame channel uses (`Converter(type_name=...)` takes the fork as an argument, so the channel is one construction away), the total is the catalogue's own $2,100, and the engine attributes the battery's fire per sample. Terrain fact worth keeping: the shore-most buildable land on the anchor-mirror line sits at 0.14, about 196 from the first water point, so the battery covers a ~150-deep band of lake from safe ground. What a bare probe cannot answer: ship contact. Unescorted at Very Hard the probe dies to aircraft before the navy exists -- the fork cannot shoot air, so the battery lives exactly as long as the champion's aa_cover stands beside it. Next per law eleven: a pilot with the champion playing and a battery knob, its card read for `owned peak` carrying the fork and any ship in `damaged_by`'s ledger before a panel is spent.

## [2026-08-14] verdict | vhsearch1 completes its rounds: the flame mirage dies in round 1, and close0-raid0 graduates on a whisper
Artifacts: `runs/vhsearch1.log` (full per-round tables), `runs/sweeps/vhsearch1-r0/` (25 matches inc. shared controls, 16 arms x 8 pairs) and `vhsearch1-r1/` (144, 8 survivors x 16 pairs); round 0 leader `close6-flame0` +2.047 (sd 2.304, n=8) COLLAPSED to +0.075 (sd 3.048, n=16) in round 1; final graduation order `close0-raid0` (+0.867, sd 2.963), `raid0` (+0.664), `tech2` (+0.240), `flame0` (+0.208); `sweeps/grad1-close0raid0.txt` submitted -- 48 fresh pairs (seeds 213001-213095 odd), champion control, the +4 bar judging.
Notes: The first machine-proposed docket ran end to end: 16 candidates, two margin-triaged rounds, one survivor nominated -- ~170 matches where the manual ladder would have spent ~280 on three arms. The methodological finding outranks any candidate: round 0's leader was a textbook 8-pair mirage carrying the seductive story ("the champion is better without its signature flamethrowers") and round 1 erased it wholesale -- successive halving caught in one cheap round what cost raid8 a retraction and a law. The strategic finding is quieter and consistent with the local-optimum reading: at n=16 every survivor sits within ~1 sem of zero, and the nominee `close0-raid0` -- notable as a REMOVAL arm, close and raid both off -- leads at barely a third of its own sd. Expectation calibrated: the panel is a coin worth flipping properly (the search's job was to pick WHICH coin), not a discovery. If the bar rejects it, the searched knob-space closes as flat around the champion, and the campaign's weight shifts to new verbs -- the battery being first in line.

## [2026-08-14] fix | the battery pilot voids itself: the flame channel eats the shore turret, and two claims learn to coexist
Artifacts: `runs/battery-pilot.log` + card (seed 424243, VH, flame-battery1): `battery:c_turret_t1 asked 1 got 1 spent 500` -- the walk stood its turret first try -- then `battery:c_turret_t1_artillery asked 236 got 0`: the $1,600 fork claim refused all match while `c_turret_t2_flame x2` appeared in the owned peak. The champion's own flame converter ($700, claiming earlier in the tick) took the shore turret each time it stood; the battery channel had become a flame-turret feeder and no artillery battery ever existed. Fixes, both regression-locked: (1) the site turret is spoken for -- both converters take an `exclude` and the quartermaster passes the battery's holder; (2) the fork CLAIMS first in the tick (its accrual must not sit behind the cheaper conversion) while its order still SENDS last. `make check` green end to end; pilot resubmitted on the fixed tree.
Notes: Law eleven pays for itself on its first post-navy application: the mechanism census caught in ONE pilot what five navy panels needed a week to surface -- the ledger said paid (500 spent, turret stood), the peak said the wrong thing existed (flame x2, battery x0), and only the two together named intra-bot channel competition as the failure. A new mechanism class for the record: not the engine stealing the builder (navy96b) or identity churn (navy96e) but two of OUR OWN channels contending for one holder with the cheaper claim structurally first. The eviction rule shipped alongside (an overridden holder no longer counts as underway) keeps the flame headcount honest in the same collision.

## [2026-08-14] fix | pilot three WINS but the fork never claims: the engine accepts after patience moves on, and the walk forgets its own offers
Artifacts: `runs/battery-pilot3.log` + card (seed 424245, VH, flame-battery1): verdict **won**, `battery:c_turret_t1 asked 1 got 1`, `c_turret_t1 x1` in the owned peak -- and zero fork claims, zero fork orders. The engine log names the flaw: orders at fraction 0.22 (1091,1656) through the patience window, then the walk moved to 0.20 (1054,1784) -- and the turret stood at a point the walk had already abandoned, 165 manhattan from the only site the channel remembered. `_own_turret` matched nothing, so the conversion half never saw a holder. Fix: the walk remembers EVERY offered point, and a structure within snap of any of them is its own; regression test drives the exact sequence (patience expiry, then late acceptance at the abandoned fraction). `make check` green; pilot five submitted on the same seed.
Notes: Third mechanism defect in three pilots, each shallower than the last -- channel competition (fix: exclusion + claim order), death mid-conversion (fix: re-funded rebuilds), and now acceptance latency (fix: site memory). The probe never met this because its acceptances were immediate; the pilot's builder was contending with the opening for path time. Law eleven's arithmetic keeps improving: each defect cost one pilot (minutes) instead of one panel (hours), and the five-panel navy era's whole lesson -- the card must prove existence, not payment -- is what made each card legible. Also on the record: the arm WON its match with the battery half-built, which measures nothing (one seed) but confirms the diversion is not fatal.

## [2026-08-14] verdict+launch | grad1 lands exactly ON the bar: close0-raid0 wins 26-22, and law nine sends it to replication

## [2026-08-14] fix | pilot five names the fourth defect: a tail-of-tick claim binds nobody, and the battery starves through 4,866 refusals
Artifacts: `runs/battery-pilot5.log` + card (seed 424245, fixed site memory): the walk's turret stood early (site fix proven -- orders stop at the abandoned-fraction acceptance) and died young; `battery:c_turret_t1 asked 4867 got 1` -- the rebuild claim refused for the rest of the match ("wanted 500 of 31 available") because the claim lived at the END of the spending chain, where the codebase's own fund_cover comment says a withhold binds nobody; the fork never asked once. Fix: funding is its own step, `Battery.fund`, called FIRST in the quartermaster's produce pass -- the finisher's early-claim precedent applied -- with establish/convert reduced to order-senders; claims split per half, re-funded per rebuild; the whole battery test file reworked around the fund/order seam. `make check` green; pilots six and seven submitted on fresh seeds.
Notes: Four defects, four pilots, and a pattern worth naming: every one was an INTERACTION with the running bot -- channel competition, mid-conversion death, acceptance latency, spending-chain position -- and none was visible in the standalone probe, whose world contained no competitor for credits, builders, or the holder. "Probes ship whole" (law eleven) got the mechanics right; what the probe could not carry was the economy around them. The cards made each defect one grep: paid-but-nothing-stood (census), stood-but-never-asked (spend table), asked-but-never-granted (the held reason).

## [2026-08-15] build | the spatial layer's first slice: coverage as a field, recorded not acted on
Artifacts: `src/rw_bot/policy/field.py` (`guns_covering` -- visible hostiles whose weapon reaches the target's layer AND whose declared range spans the distance now; `coverage` -- both directions in one pass) + three trace columns appended after `events` per the positional-reader rule: `eco_covered` (finished extractors under hostile guns), `own_covered` (everything of ours under hostile guns), `foe_covered` (hostiles inside OUR guns' reach -- the pair is the engagement balance). Computed by the Recorder itself, the income-pair precedent; the extractor family is passed in from the same `TIER_CHAINS` the scoreboard counts, so the column and the scoreboard cannot drift apart. All gates green; verified live the same hour (`runs/field-pilot.trace`, seed 424255, 4,000 samples, survived): zeros through the quiet opening, first contact at row 706 -- `foe_covered` first, our guns reaching their vanguard before theirs reached us -- 2,521 rows of contact, peaks eco 2 / own 13 / foe 13.
Notes: Scoped by the campaign's own laws rather than by ambition. Law five (input without a gated response is perturbation, twice measured): the first consumer is the TRACE, a recording no rule reads, so nothing about play changes and nothing can regress. Law ten (measure the response before the trigger): positioning consumers -- hold v2 siting, threat-aware gathering -- will read these same functions when one is designed and priced, not before. The build reuses [[policy-threat]]'s legitimacy spine wholesale (engine hostility, engine layer test, registry reach; standing positions only, no closure model) and generalizes it from one route to every unit both ways. The raster grid is deliberately NOT built yet: every current consumer asks about points that exist (our units, their units), and a raster pays only when a consumer searches over empty ground for a place to stand -- that consumer's arm buys the raster. What this feeds first: the next doom-model refit, whose ninety features contain counts and rates and not one bit of WHERE -- every control half of every future panel now accumulates spatial columns for free.

## [2026-08-15] build | make sources: the wiki's mechanical contract becomes a gate, and its first run catches the auditor
Artifacts: `scripts/wiki_check.py` + `tests/test_wiki_check.py` (fixture trees breaking every rule once) + the `sources` target, a prerequisite of `make check`. Six fatal checks: source paths resolve with line anchors in bounds (URLs skipped), pinned blob paths exist, wikilinks land on a page or hub, footnotes pair in both directions, every page is hub-reachable, and the index's per-hub and total counts match reality. Blob DRIFT is deliberately not gated -- a stale pin means "nobody re-read this page", and gating it rewards bumping pins without re-reading (the sibling client's own split of rule from report).
Notes: The question that forced it -- "make sources passes?" -- had the honest answer NO SUCH TARGET: this client's guard was lifted from the sibling without its wiki rules, and the discipline lived in two hand-run session scripts. The gate's very first run against the real tree then caught the hand audit itself: every hub carries a format-example comment whose fake link the ad-hoc substring count included, so all five index counts committed hours earlier were inflated by one. A checker that finds a bug in the process that built it is the whole argument for mechanizing the process.
