## [2026-08-30] verdict | the replication panel runs on HPC3, and cross-invocation replay does NOT hold under the Linux depot's Java 8
Artifacts: `runs/traces/replicate-r1/` (round 1, 24 members), `runs/traces/replicate/` (round 2, three pairs re-run); cluster copies at `/pub/wagnera3/rusted/runs/round1/`; campaign `clients/RustedWarfareBot/provenance/replicate-campaign.json`; image `rusted.sif` sha256 `d194314c892645f05c6d50ea03b554960fa4081d6e3fcaffa07eba24e8667541`
Notes: The panel `sweeps/replicate.txt` -- twelve seeds, each played twice under two labels -- ran as 24 scheduled jobs inside a digest-pinned apptainer image, realtime, against the staged Linux depot (JRE 1.8.0_131). It exists because the tick-bracket regime was certified on 2026-08-07 entirely on this Windows workstation under the depot's Java 13, and no number produced on the cluster is under a checked regime until this passes there. **It does not pass.** Round 1: 8/12 pairs bit-identical over 250 samples; seeds 8128, 31337 and 90210 forked at frames 7125/7050/7125 (samples 94-95), seed 24601 forked at frame 375 (sample 5) on `credits` 4009-vs-4013 with `income` equal in both. Round 2 re-ran the three late-forking pairs with their clones deleted so they rebuilt fresh: 8128 and 90210 came back IDENTICAL, 31337 forked again at frame 7050. **The fork is therefore not seed-determined**, and the per-member comparison across rounds is the sharper result: `repB-s8128` and `repB-s90210` reproduced themselves BIT-EXACT across two invocations forty minutes apart on different nodes under wholly different load, while `repA` of both moved onto repB's trajectory (fork -> identical), and both members of 31337 moved. So cross-invocation reproduction is achievable on this runtime and simply does not always happen -- which is the 2026-08-07 mechanism exactly ("one draw routed differently shifts the sim stream; the world forks at the next behavioral roll"), on a stack where `TickBracket` was supposed to have removed the last per-process input. Frame 7050 is where divergence SURFACES on duel_lake, not where it originates (the 2026-08-07 entry says so directly), so seeds clustering at 7050-7125 is the map's own consequential roll and NOT evidence of a shared cause. Seed 24601 is a separate signature and is confounded: its two members' runtimes differed by 307s (283s alone vs 590s inside a 22-way burst) because repA was re-run after a startup failure, so it is excluded from the mechanism claim above. What this does NOT establish: whether the variable is Java 8 or the cluster environment -- the two changed together, and this panel cannot separate them. The cheap discriminator is a forked pair run locally in Docker under the Linux depot's Java 8, which costs no allocation; the instrument for the cause is the draw tap (`PLAY_RNGTAP=1`), with the caveat the 2026-08-07 entry records against its per-window methodology at fast-forward. Harness findings paid for on the way, both fixed and staged: the frozen tree did not carry the two registry dumps the planner reads (`printunits.log`, `type-flags.ndjson`) and the first member to reach the planner died on `FileNotFoundError: 'wiki/sources/m0-probe/printunits.log'` having already patched, seeded and held the world at frame one; and every member leased clone ordinal 1, resolving a relative `.game-w1` against the directory members are SUBMITTED from, so two nodes nine seconds apart aimed at one directory and the second died listing a maps tree the first had not finished copying (jobs 55663569/55663571). A member now takes `--clones` and derives its lease from its position in the job file. One member was also lost to the 90s port wait while the engine was still loading mods -- a 56s asset read under 22-way contention against 4ms on the other twenty-three -- which is a startup budget keyed on a clock rather than on progress, and is not yet fixed.

## [2026-08-30] rootcause-progress | the fork is NOT the cluster: it reproduces on one workstation, and the world at frame 7050 is BISTABLE
Artifacts: `runs/traces/docker-j8/` (two separate `docker run` invocations, seed 31337); compare `runs/traces/replicate-r1/` and `runs/traces/replicate/`; container rendered from `tools/hpc3/specs/rusted-image.json`
Notes: The open question left by the panel entry above -- Java 8 or the cluster, which changed together -- is closed in one direction. A container was built from the SAME image spec the cluster's `rusted.sif` was built from (same digest-pinned base, same fourteen pinned OS packages, same `typing_extensions` pin, same three wheels) and the same two archives were extracted into it, verified by tree digest: game `dd9148e4dc78d1b5...`, payload `e0f00a566a6695c4...`, both byte-identical to what is staged on HPC3, running the depot's own `java version "1.8.0_131"`. Seed 31337 was played as TWO SEPARATE `docker run` invocations -- separate deliberately, because within-batch replicas always agreed in the 2026-08-07 hunt and only separate invocations forked, twin processes sharing a JIT timeline. **It forked, at sample 94, frame 7050 -- the same sample and the same frame as on the cluster.** So the cluster is not the variable: no scheduler, no BeeGFS, no contention, one quiet machine, same result. **And the world at that roll is BISTABLE.** Across six invocations on two machines with different hardware, kernel and filesystem, the digest at frame 7050 takes exactly two values -- `4207680129` and `1822014061` -- and never a third; HPC3 r1 read A/B, HPC3 r2 read B/A, Docker read B/A. A machine-independent two-valued state reached by a per-invocation coin flip is the signature of ONE binary decision routed differently, which is the 2026-08-07 mechanism verbatim on a stack where `TickBracket` was meant to have removed the last per-process input. What this buys: the hunt moves off the cluster entirely. A local invocation is ~8 minutes and costs no allocation, so N invocations give a distribution over the two states rather than a single pass/fail, and the draw tap can be iterated at workstation speed. What is still NOT established: whether the routing input is the Java 8 runtime itself or something else common to the Linux stack -- the Windows depot's Java 13 has never been run against this same panel, and that comparison is the remaining arm of the discriminator. Frame 7050 remains where divergence SURFACES on duel_lake, not where it originates; the upstream desync (`RandomLedger` showed engine-stream states parting at frame 150 in the pre-bracket work) is where the bisect belongs.

## [2026-08-30] rootcause+fix | two of three generators were never split, and the third seam is a wall-paced spawner inside the tick
Artifacts: `runs/traces/{replicate-r1,replicate}` (HPC3 rounds 1-2), `runs/traces/{docker-j8,fix,fix3,count,tap2,pin,pinv}` (local, Java 8 container built from `tools/hpc3/specs/rusted-image.json`); agent `SplitRandom.installMath/installShuffle`, `EngineRandom.swapMathGenerator/swapShuffleGenerator`, `RandomChecks`
Notes: The panel's forks ran to ground, and the answer was not the runtime. **Three generators feed the simulation and the 2026-08-07 tick split closed exactly one.** `EngineRandom.seed` pins the engine's generator, `Math.random`'s and `Collections.shuffle`'s alike, and seeding makes a run START from a known state while saying nothing about whether the simulation's draws from it are a function of the seed. Math's holder is JVM-global with twelve engine call sites reading it, so the render path and the simulation shared one stream drawn at moments only the scheduler chooses. The RandomLedger had said so all along: across eleven seeds replayed on HPC3, every seed whose `math=` state agreed at frame 0 replicated bit-exact over 250 samples and every seed whose `math=` state differed at frame 0 forked, with the engine stream diverging only AFTER the world already had (8128: engine 7200/math 0; 31337: 8175/0; 90210: 7950/0; eight clean seeds all `same`). **Splitting Math alone moved the fork EARLIER, and that is how the third leak surfaced:** shuffle had agreed across all eleven seeds only because the Math leak forked the world before shuffle could matter; split Math and shuffle became the first stream to diverge, at frame 600. A leak is invisible until the louder one above it closes, which is the reading trap this whole arc turned on -- "the fix made it worse" was progress being misread. With all three split, seed 31337 (which forked 3/3 before any change) went four trajectories -> two clusters -> three of four invocations bit-identical, fork s44 -> s116 -> s198. **The remaining seam is not a generator.** `SplitRandom.onSimDraw` now counts, and the counts separate cause from consequence: two runs with bit-identical worlds for all 250 samples differed by TEN engine sim-draws at frame 300 -- draws taken inside the tick whose values nothing in the world reads. The draw tap named the call site: `com.corrodinggames.rts.game.units.y.a(am,float,int)` lines 11504-11505, two `Math.random()` calls scattering an effect position by +-8 world units, gated on a per-unit float accumulator decremented by the MEASURED frame delta (`U = f.a(U, delta); if (U == 0) { U = 5.0; ...}`), with a sibling `y.b` of identical shape -- the same pattern as the ambient spawner already silenced, on the unit class rather than the effects manager. **The engine's own delta kill switch does NOT close it, and that result is now trustworthy:** `pinDeltaMs` writes `bu` silently, so an unarmed run was indistinguishable from an armed one; it logs now (`frame delta pinned to 3ms (0.17999999 steps) via bu`) and three invocations with the pin confirmed armed still forked -- one of them taking ZERO shuffle sim-draws by frame 600 where the others took 11, which is a conditional path rather than a shifted stream. So the last seam is multi-headed and open. **The fix is a decision, not a discovery:** neutralising those draws is unlike every existing entry in `Targets`, which is scoped to render-path callbacks touching no simulation state "which is what keeps the work multiplayer-legal" -- these draws come from the stream real multiplayer clients also consume. Patch `y.a`/`y.b` on seeded matches only, park the per-unit accumulator, or route those draws to the side stream: the three differ exactly in how much non-vanilla behaviour the project will accept. Guarded meanwhile: `RandomChecks` asserts sim-phase and render-phase draws come from different streams, that render draws do not move the simulation's stream, and that `setSeed` re-pins it -- the middle one being the property Math.random lacked. Corollary for the cluster: one match per job makes EVERY comparison cross-invocation, so HPC3 did not introduce this, it removed what was hiding it.
